# spec/

`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` is draft **0.2.12** (2026-11-01), from eight consumer-named gaps,
awaiting council review. It supersedes accepted draft 0.2.11 (round 10, sol/codex);
accepted 0.2.10 is retained in git, with binding interpretations in
[`errata-0.2.10.md`](errata-0.2.10.md). Binding interpretations of 0.2.12 itself (text and SHA
unchanged) are in [`errata-0.2.12.md`](errata-0.2.12.md). The reviewer disposition table is in
[`../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md`](../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md).
It targets
ISA 0.8 (`../bennett-isa-spec/spec/`, settled) and is graded against it. `bennett_markers.h` is the
normative C/C++ header of the marker ABI (section 3.3). `Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.2.PROGRESS.md` is
the writing job's log. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett` meta repo
(`docs/council/`, `tools/council.py`); the rubric is `docs/council/rubric-isa.md`, under which any
narrowing of north-star scope is S0.

Rules for changing it:

- **No narrowing** (CLAUDE.md rule 10). A compile-time rejection needs a row in section 22.1 citing
  the north-star clause or maintainer decision that requires it. A deferral (section 24) postpones
  mechanics only and states the capability it keeps. A capability ISA 0.8 cannot express is a
  blocker (section 25.1) with the exact ISA change and a `blocked:` verdict, never a rejection.
- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (header and section 23); drafts are not
  compatible with each other. The reference objects and hashes of section 16 depend on the ISA
  version string, the lifting map and the layout, so they change with any of them.
- **Checked examples** (section 1.5, process rules P-1 to P-3):
  - P-1: every rule id has an `accept` example, every rejection a `reject` example, every blocker
    a `blocked` example; Appendix B is the index, and a rule without one fails the check.
  - P-2: an example the checker cannot yet execute fails (`checker-unimplemented: <capability>`);
    no example is removed or weakened to make the checker pass; the freeze gate is all green.
  - P-3: executed results come from independent executors (LLVM tools, native binaries, the Rust
    ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`); Python computes expected artefacts only.
- Run `python3 scripts/dialect_check_v02.py` before anyone grades this draft; it must exit 0. (For 0.2 the
  checker is being extended against the Rust interpreter in a separate job; until then the 0.2
  examples were executed by the writing job as its progress file records.)
- Add every new example or stated result as a tagged block, so the checker executes it; an
  untagged `llvm` or `isa` block is an error. A `semantics=` example must be byte-equal to its
  `corpus/legality/cases/spec-*.ll` file; run `python3 corpus/legality/harness/run.py baseline
  --majors 18,20,23 --cases 'cases/spec-*'` (headless, CLAUDE.md rule 11) after changing one.
- Write derivations out before stating numbers; every stated example result must be computed.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every time it was tried (ISA
  rounds 1-8).
- Revisions go back through the council.
# Binding interpretations of dialect 0.2.12

Published 2026-11-02. Text of draft 0.2.12 at `c775e6d458006fde143f2bf8ec62f07b034fb431`, spec SHA-256 `cfcce70e84ead8a05924c0921f13fc9cb477fdd71f5448c7f6f357ea91349e83`, is unchanged; the full version string, map 0.2.9 and consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations clarify that text without changing any reference result. Draft 0.3 carries the same text in §§5.1, 5.2 and 24.

## 5u3 — §§5.1, 5.2, 24 (council run `contract-5u3-2026-11-02`, round 1 sol and codex accept; `accept-4ht-recheck-2026-11-02` round 1 PASS; muse accept in `accept-muse-batch-2026-11-02`; bead `b-dialect-spec-5u3`)

**§5.1, after K.** The reference bounds INT atoms as above; it imposes no additional
cardinality bound on pointer-offset sets or memory intervals. Implementers
MAY use exact symbolic sets, shared interval relations and SCC
acceleration instead of enumerating represented offsets or slots.
All reference sets and byte/representation-width distinctions must be
preserved for verdicts, candidate sets and dispatch domains. A coarser
summary is not the reference result merely because it is sound for
aliasing. An unfinished analysis reports an unfinished build with the
rule and exhausted implementation resource, not a source rejection,
completed verdict or run-time fault for a valid input.

**§5.2, after the least-fixpoint paragraph.** In a pure modular-translation inclusion SCC, if every cycle transfer is
an unfiltered fixed displacement at one bit width W and preserves one
base/provenance coordinate, close the generated displacements as a
subgroup of Z/(2^W). For a single self-cycle of stride s, each seed a
denotes the coset a modulo gcd(s,2^W). Union distinct seed cosets. For
multiple vertices, retain vertex-specific path displacements; do not
merge their seed coordinates. For edges u→v labelled d_uv,
choose root-to-vertex path displacements p_v and let
g=gcd(2^W,{p_u+d_uv−p_v}). A seed a at u contributes
{a−p_u+p_v+kg mod 2^W} at v. Union contributions from all seeds.
Apply the same rule to a memory relation
only when every transfer preserves the complete storage interval and
representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
to the represented individual slots. Retain every ROOT and FN alternative
explicitly. Otherwise use the original rules or another exact method.
PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
this rule does not replace them by unrestricted full-pointer addition.

**§24.** Analysis representation/acceleration is implementation policy; the
default result is the exact §5 least fixpoint, including existing K=4096
INT abstraction. Time or memory caps do not alter that result. A report
of unfinished analysis keeps the remaining implementation obligation open.
# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1024:| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1025:| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1026:| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1027:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1028:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1029:| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1030:| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1031:| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1033:| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4788:| N15 | `extractelement`/`insertelement` with any integer index i on L lanes | interpret iK index i as unsigned, including negative spellings. A constant i<L is left for N21’s in-range lane renaming. A constant i>=L replaces all result uses by the zero element (extract) or the unchanged input vector (insert), then deletes the instruction. For a non-constant index: let W=max(K,⌈log2(L+1)⌉), zero-extend to `%nz.i.r` when W>K (otherwise use i unchanged); `%nz.c.r = icmp uge iW wide_i, L` with exact L; `%nz.e.r = <the original instruction>`; `%r = select i1 %nz.c.r, T Z, T %nz.e.r` with Z = the zero element (extract) or the unchanged vector operand (insert) |

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6797:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6803:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6844:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1016:### 5.2 Rules
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1041:representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1044:PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1049:| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1050:| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1051:| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1052:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1053:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1054:| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1055:| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1056:| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1057:| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1058:| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1059:| PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6 |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1060:| PT12 | a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call; a call of a function with no definition that resolves to a host call (§4.5) or is trapped; an `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg` (before N9) | `qalloc`/`qborrow`: AV ∋ ROOT(site) (§6.5). An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤. Before domains freeze, PT12 MUST apply PT6 to the old value, the PT5 transfers of the full N9 update, and PT7 to the actual stored value, with cmpxchg returning its old-value and success fields and storing the selected new-or-old value. [deps: DLT-5.2-P1, DLT-5.2-P6, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, DLT-5.2-P13, DLT-12.2-P11, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2] |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1067:**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1137:**PT12 spelling witnesses (non-normative).** Explicit address spaces, volatile/weak qualifiers, syncscope and alignment retain the same N9 value effects and native materialised outcomes; the pointer operand keeps its address-space type in the generated load and store.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1185:PT12 checked ROOT propagation: `make` returns the fresh qalloc ROOT to its caller;

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:4809:| N15 | `extractelement`/`insertelement` with any integer index i on L lanes | interpret iK index i as unsigned, including negative spellings. A constant i<L is left for N21’s in-range lane renaming. A constant i>=L replaces all result uses by the zero element (extract) or the unchanged input vector (insert), then deletes the instruction. For a non-constant index: let W=max(K,⌈log2(L+1)⌉), zero-extend to `%nz.i.r` when W>K (otherwise use i unchanged); `%nz.c.r = icmp uge iW wide_i, L` with exact L; `%nz.e.r = <the original instruction>`; `%r = select i1 %nz.c.r, T Z, T %nz.e.r` with Z = the zero element (extract) or the unchanged vector operand (insert) |

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6818:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6824:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6865:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:8732:**`indirectbr-two-blocks`.** The two address-taken blocks have values B(f)=4096 and B(f)+1=4097. The address object has two bytes. Measured by bennett-plugin bp-fj1 on the rung-4 closure (N=16, 1,487 functions, LLVM 18): without a bound, 800k+ slots after round 1 and still growing. # 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

**PT3 geometry-changing bitcast witnesses (DB/DN/DS).** On this little-endian
layout, <1,1> packs to 1 | (1 << 8) = 257, and <0,1> packs to 256.
Each result is one scalar INT atom, never two source-lane atoms. The inverse
probe splits 769 = 1 + (3 << 8) into <1,3>, then selects 3. All three
are accepted with finite dispatch; no BLK-1 applies. The state checks verify
source O0, instcombine and virtual-N21 materialisation on LLVM 18/23, then
execute the source and materialised forms with native width-domain guards at
O0/O3. The first flips wire 256 twice; every allocation frees cleanly, code 0.

```llvm example=pt3-pack257 verdict=accept
target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"

 %v0 = insertelement <2 x i8> zeroinitializer, i8 1, i32 0
 %v1 = insertelement <2 x i8> %v0, i8 1, i32 1
 %bits = bitcast <2 x i8> %v1 to i16
 %n = zext i16 %bits to i64
 %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %n)
 call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 256)
 call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 256)
 call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
 ret void
}
```

**PT10 totalised lane domain.** The dynamic extraction from <1,3>
has domain {0,1,3}, so adding one gives finite width dispatch {1,2,4}.
Indices 0,1,2,-1 allocate 2,4,1,1 wires and free cleanly; no precondition

**Pointer-integer wrap witnesses.** Two i64 additions of −2^63 have net
zero displacement modulo 2^64, so p=@tab and f=@body. Modular displacement
alternatives are retained alongside unknown-address alternatives, never lost at

inttoptr to a 64-bit pointer likewise returns @tab. The third witness computes
the address of a different allocation: unknown-address loading must retain its
possible function value. With adequate budgets dispatch succeeds exactly once.

A constant-byte fill carries its complete filled interval and byte value; a load
contained in that interval decodes the repeated byte at the load's width. Other
overlapping writes still participate in PT6 (a fill does not erase them).
PT8 copies preserve storage intervals/representation widths only for fully copied
atoms. Partial copies and fills add overlapping affected intervals; PT6 then returns
⊤ for any load whose whole bits are not represented exactly. These are fixed
reference rules, not optional precision improvements.

Handle-array propagation MUST retain field intervals and finite-width address images so that a disjoint integer counter is not handle-derived, without treating an array type as a proof that indices stay in bounds. [deps: DLT-5.1-P7, DLT-5.2-P3, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2]

**Compatible allocation/collection witness (non-normative).** A defined wrapper
returns an attributed fresh allocation whose name is not a built-in allocator.
A two-level heap header carries a handle through a shared global; a disjoint
counter remains ordinary integer data. Two indirect candidates return different
ROOTs. An i1 vector index selects exactly one live lane without an out-of-range
zero alternative. Both inputs execute four X gates, two clean frees and code0;
LLVM18/23 source and materialised O0/O3 execute independently of the analysis.

```llvm example=compatible-allocator-graph verdict=accept callees=first,second

@observed_old = global i64 0
@observed_success = global i64 0

  %link = getelementptr i8, ptr %node, i64 8
  store ptr null, ptr %link
  ret ptr %node
}

  ret ptr %a
}

  ret ptr %b
}
define void @host_count() {
  %counter = getelementptr { ptr, i64 }, ptr @shared, i64 0, i32 1
  store i64 9, ptr %counter
  ret void
}
define void @top(i1 %choose) {
  %a = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  %b = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  %node = call ptr @make_node(ptr %a)
  %header = call ptr @custom_alloc(i64 8)

  store ptr %header, ptr @shared
  %loaded_header = load ptr, ptr @shared

  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %from_callback, i64 0)

  %count = load i64, ptr %counter
```

### 5.3 Value sets

The **value set** VS(v) of an integer or pointer operand is the set of its `INT` and `FN` atoms
(pointer atoms denote addresses that are not compile-time constants and make VS(v) = ⊤ for
dispatch purposes), or ⊤. A value set is a precision tool only:

- an operand with a finite VS is dispatched over it (§3.4, §14.2);
- an operand with VS = ⊤ is dispatched over its type's whole valid domain where that domain is
  finite: operation codes and classes (§3.4), binding kinds (§8.4), the function addresses of a
  type (§7.2, §14.2);
- otherwise it is a blocker case of §25.1: widths (BLK-1), budgets computed on the device
  (BLK-2), parameters of in-place pairs (§8.7).

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6916:For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6920:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6955:canonical encoding, with signed zeros preserved where the operation requires.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6978:**Accuracy envelope.** For each finite mathematical result of a §13.4 own-format carrier transcendental, the implementation MUST return a B value within one local ulp of that exact result, over the entire finite input domain; local ulp is the spacing of B's adjacent finite values bracketing the exact result, taking an exactly representable result as the lower endpoint (the upper endpoint at the largest finite value), with the least positive subnormal spacing at zero or in the subnormal interval. For ppcf128 use §13.1's ordered canonical-pair values. Special values, domain errors, poles, signed zeros and overflow results follow the named operation's mathematical/IEEE semantics rather than this finite-result envelope. At overflow the result is the correctly rounded overflow outcome for the requested mode. This is an error bound, not a general correct-rounding claim.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6831:| `llvm.sqrt`, `llvm.floor`, `llvm.ceil`, `llvm.trunc`, `llvm.round`, `llvm.roundeven`, `llvm.rint`, `llvm.nearbyint`, `llvm.canonicalize` | `C @__bennett_sf_<name>_B(C)` | bitcast |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6842:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6844:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6857:intervenes. For canonical temporary slots, emit ss_push(8L,8), then limb
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6871:observable flags or exceptions (except §13.5); **every NaN result is the format's canonical NaN**
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6874:the f64 canonical NaN in the high double and +0 in the low); `rem` is C `fmod` (exact, the sign of
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6880:operand; two: the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimum`/`maximum` are IEEE 754-2019 (either NaN:
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6881:the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimumnum`/`maximumnum` are IEEE 754-2019 minimumNumber/
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6884:nearest even; `canonicalize` maps every NaN to the canonical NaN and is the identity otherwise;
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6895:For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6899:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6934:canonical encoding, with signed zeros preserved where the operation requires.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6957:**Accuracy envelope.** For each finite mathematical result of a §13.4 own-format carrier transcendental, the implementation MUST return a B value within one local ulp of that exact result, over the entire finite input domain; local ulp is the spacing of B's adjacent finite values bracketing the exact result, taking an exactly representable result as the lower endpoint (the upper endpoint at the largest finite value), with the least positive subnormal spacing at zero or in the subnormal interval. For ppcf128 use §13.1's ordered canonical-pair values. Special values, domain errors, poles, signed zeros and overflow results follow the named operation's mathematical/IEEE semantics rather than this finite-result envelope. At overflow the result is the correctly rounded overflow outcome for the requested mode. This is an error bound, not a general correct-rounding claim.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6981:Both entries MUST compute the same LLVM predicate truth value, while quiet `cmp` raises invalid only for a signaling NaN operand and signaling `cmps` raises invalid for either quiet or signaling NaN, even for predicates false/true; neither raises divbyzero, overflow, underflow or inexact, and no comparison canonicalises or modifies its input bits.

3. in a CC variant only, a **host call** through `hostcall` (§11.4);
4. otherwise **trapped**: the call is replaced by F(8) (§12.3) and the coverage report has a

   never silent").

An R or L variant never makes a host call (ISA §4.1: `hostcall` is CC only; NS §4.2: coherent
regions are closed). *(non-normative)* Item 4 is the only fault the dialect introduces for a
call, and it occurs only on the executions that reach the call; it is the north star's own rule.

## 5. The device analysis (one fixpoint)

The device analysis is a whole-program, flow-insensitive, inclusion-based (Andersen-style),
field-sensitive points-to and value-set analysis over every device variant and every host
function that exchanges data with one (passes it a pointer, or shares a global it accesses). It
is specified exactly: **every verdict, candidate set and dispatch domain of this document is
computed from the reference analysis below.** A plugin MAY use a more precise analysis to shrink
dispatch domains or arena placement in its lowering, never to change a verdict (the run-time
value is always a member of the reference set, so the results are unchanged).

### 5.1 Abstract objects and atoms

The **abstract objects** are:

- each `alloca` instruction;
- each global variable;
- each **allocation site**: a call of a function that carries `allockind("alloc")` or
  `allocsize(...)`, or whose name is one of `malloc`, `calloc`, `realloc`, `aligned_alloc`,
  `posix_memalign` (the object is the one written through its first argument), `memalign`,
  `strdup`, `strndup`, `_Znwm`, `_Znam`, `_ZnwmRKSt9nothrow_t`, `_ZnamRKSt9nothrow_t`,
  `_ZnwmSt11align_val_t`, `_ZnamSt11align_val_t`, `__rust_alloc`, `__rust_alloc_zeroed`,
  `__rust_realloc`, `julia.gc_alloc_obj`, `ijl_gc_alloc_typed`, `jl_gc_alloc`,
  `ijl_gc_small_alloc`, `ijl_gc_big_alloc`, or `__bennett_nz_alloc`;
- the pseudo-object `EXT` (memory of unknown provenance: host memory reached through a pointer
  the analysis did not create).

Heap objects are context-insensitive: one object per allocation-site instruction of the variant
in which it occurs. The list above is the reference list; `bennett-runtimes` declares its
allocator entry points with `allockind("alloc")` so that they are covered by the first rule.

An **offset** is an integer byte offset, a **residue class** (r mod s)[lo, hi) with s ≥ 2 (every
offset o with o ≡ r (mod s) and lo ≤ o < hi), or `*` (any offset). An **atom** is one of:

- `(X, o)`: a pointer into object X at offset o;
- `FN(F)`: the address A(F) of a defined function F;
- `ROOT(r)`: a handle with root r (the allocation site of a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call, §6.5);
- `INT(c)`: the integer constant c (of the value's width).

Every SSA value v (per lane for vectors, per index path for aggregates) has an **abstract
value** AV(v) = (A, t): a finite set A of atoms and a flag t; t = ⊤ means "and any other integer
or address". ⊤ never stands for a `ROOT` atom (handle provenance is always explicit); for a
function-pointer operand it stands for "any function of the operand's type whose address is
used" (§7.2, §14.2). Every slot (X, o) of memory has a content AV(X, o) of the same form. When A
holds more than **K = 4096** `INT` atoms they are dropped and t is set (a normative constant: it
decides dispatch and blocker verdicts, so §24 does not defer it). "AV(v) = ⊤" below means t is
set; unions and inclusions are taken component-wise.

Abstract-object and return propagation MUST cover attributed allocator calls and defined allocator wrappers through their underlying allocation sites without requiring the wrapper's name in a whitelist. [deps: DLT-5.1-P4, DLT-5.1-P6, DLT-5.2-P10, ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2]

### 5.2 Rules

Before the fixpoint, seed the initialiser atoms of every global, mutable or constant,
into its slots. For each alloca and allocation-site object, also represent every extent
whose initial contents are zero under §§17.1/17.3 as a zero-byte initial image; represent a
nonconstant extent symbolically over its possible allocated bytes. Subsequent stores union
additional possibilities and never erase these initial possibilities. This is an initial
image, not a claim that the object remains immutable.
The analysis is the least fixpoint of the following rules over AV. "Copies" means AV(result) ⊇
AV(operand) lane by lane; ⊤ absorbs. S2 models the value effects of the specified
normalisation before verdicts and dispatch domains freeze; pre-normalisation syntax does
not permit omitting a value produced by the materialised execution.

| Rule | Instruction or source | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |
| PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6 |
| PT12 | a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call; a call of a function with no definition that resolves to a host call (§4.5) or is trapped; an `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg` (before N9) | `qalloc`/`qborrow`: AV ∋ ROOT(site) (§6.5). An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤. Before domains freeze, PT12 MUST apply PT6 to the old value, the PT5 transfers of the full N9 update, and PT7 to the actual stored value, with cmpxchg returning its old-value and success fields and storing the selected new-or-old value. [deps: DLT-5.2-P1, DLT-5.2-P6, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, DLT-5.2-P13, DLT-12.2-P11, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2] |

An `inttoptr` of a constant gives (EXT, `*`) and, for function-pointer purposes, ⊤: function
addresses are symbolic `FN` atoms during the analysis, and their numeric values A(F) are fixed
only by the comparison link (§19.5), after normalisation (S7) and before lifting (S8).


**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.

```llvm example=pt12-xor verdict=accept
@cell = internal global i64 1
@observed_old = global i64 0
@observed_success = global i64 0
define void @top() {
  %old = atomicrmw xor ptr @cell, i64 2 seq_cst
  store i64 %old, ptr @observed_old
  %width = load i64, ptr @cell
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %width)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}
```

```text state=pt12-xor at=return
{"domains": {"%old": [1, 3], "%width": [1, 3]}, "native": [1, 0, 3, 1, 0, 0]}
```

```llvm example=pt12-cmpxchg-success verdict=accept
@cell = internal global i64 1
@observed_old = global i64 0
@observed_success = global i64 0
define void @top() {
  %pair = cmpxchg ptr @cell, i64 1, i64 3 seq_cst seq_cst
  %old = extractvalue { i64, i1 } %pair, 0
  %ok = extractvalue { i64, i1 } %pair, 1
  store i64 %old, ptr @observed_old
  %ok64 = zext i1 %ok to i64
  store i64 %ok64, ptr @observed_success
  %width = load i64, ptr @cell
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %width)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}
```

```text state=pt12-cmpxchg-success at=return
{"domains": {"%ok": [0, 1], "%old": [1, 3], "%width": [1, 3]}, "native": [1, 1, 3, 1, 0, 0]}
```

```llvm example=pt12-cmpxchg-failure verdict=accept
@cell = internal global i64 1
@observed_old = global i64 0
@observed_success = global i64 0
define void @top() {
  %pair = cmpxchg ptr @cell, i64 9, i64 3 seq_cst seq_cst
  %old = extractvalue { i64, i1 } %pair, 0
  %ok = extractvalue { i64, i1 } %pair, 1
  store i64 %old, ptr @observed_old
  %ok64 = zext i1 %ok to i64
  store i64 %ok64, ptr @observed_success
  %width = load i64, ptr @cell
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %width)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}
```

```text state=pt12-cmpxchg-failure at=return
{"domains": {"%ok": [0], "%old": [1], "%width": [1]}, "native": [1, 0, 1, 1, 0, 0]}
```

**PT12 spelling witnesses (non-normative).** Explicit address spaces, volatile/weak qualifiers, syncscope and alignment retain the same N9 value effects and native materialised outcomes; the pointer operand keeps its address-space type in the generated load and store.

```llvm example=pt12-xor-as1 verdict=accept
@cell = internal global i64 1
@observed_old = global i64 0
@observed_success = global i64 0
define void @top() {
  %p = addrspacecast ptr @cell to ptr addrspace(1)
  %old = atomicrmw volatile xor ptr addrspace(1) %p, i64 2 syncscope("singlethread") seq_cst, align 8
  store i64 %old, ptr @observed_old
  %width = load i64, ptr @cell
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %width)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %h, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}
```

The **value set** VS(v) of an integer or pointer operand is the set of its `INT` and `FN` atoms
(pointer atoms denote addresses that are not compile-time constants and make VS(v) = ⊤ for
dispatch purposes), or ⊤. A value set is a precision tool only:

- an operand with a finite VS is dispatched over it (§3.4, §14.2);
- an operand with VS = ⊤ is dispatched over its type's whole valid domain where that domain is
  finite: operation codes and classes (§3.4), binding kinds (§8.4), the function addresses of a
  type (§7.2, §14.2);
- otherwise it is a blocker case of §25.1: widths (BLK-1), budgets computed on the device
  (BLK-2), parameters of in-place pairs (§8.7).

### 5.4 Uses

| Result | Read by |
|---|---|
| `FN` atoms of region operands | region candidate sets (§7.2) |
| `FN` atoms of indirect callees | indirect-call candidate sets (§14.2) |
| `ROOT` atoms | handle provenance (§6.2), handle containment (§6.4), static register files (§6.5) |
| `INT` atoms of descriptor and budget fields | descriptor shapes (§8.4), budget pre-minting (§9.5) |
| slots stored under control | the may-Co analysis and arena placement (§10, TDL-2, TDL-4) |
| objects loaded by L code | the oracle read set R and entry-state transfer (§8.5) |
| `INT` atoms of `op`, `cls`, `n` | dispatch domains (§3.4) |

```llvm example=pt-variable-index verdict=accept
%struct.tab = type { [4 x ptr], i64 }

define void @t(i64 %i) {
  %s = alloca %struct.tab
  %h0 = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  store ptr %h0, ptr %s
  %pn = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 1
  store i64 3, ptr %pn
  %k = and i64 %i, 3
  %pk = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 0, i64 %k
  %hk = load ptr, ptr %pk
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %hk, i64 0)
  %n = load i64, ptr %pn
  %n2 = add i64 %n, 1
  store i64 %n2, ptr %pn
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h0)
  ret void
}
```

*(non-normative)* In `pt-variable-index`, `%pk` has the offset class (0 mod 8)[0, 32) in `%s`
(the `[4 x ptr]` array), so the load `%hk` reads the slots 0, 8, 16, 24: AV(`%hk`) =
{ROOT(`%h0`)}. The count field is at the exact offset 32, outside the class, so AV(`%n`) holds
INT(3) and, through the store of `%n2`, INT(4), INT(5), … until it exceeds K and becomes ⊤; it
holds no `ROOT` atom, and the arithmetic on it is legal (audit B-27: 0.1 gave the load offset `*`
and rejected this program).

**Finite-width and materialised-domain witnesses (round 6).** Under I=32,
2^32 truncates to zero, so the table load yields FN(body) and dispatch succeeds.
For the ranged inputs, N3/N4 erase the annotations: `n & 3` gives {0,1,2,3}
and `| 1` gives {1,3}. At n=2 width 3 admits both X gates on wire 2 and a clean
free. No new input precondition or fault is introduced.
