## 6. Handles (handles 0.2.8)

### 6.1 Representation

A handle is an ordinary `ptr` in address space 0. In device memory and in integers a handle is
its **register tag**: an opaque value below 2^pw assigned to an allocation generation
(§6.5), nonzero and distinct from every identity still observable through live or stale
copies. Physical slots are reusable storage, never allocation identity. A tag MUST NOT
be recycled while an observable stale copy can remain; integer wrap is not permission
to alias generations. Copies continue to denote their original generation after free. ISA 0.8 has no instruction that
reads a `qreg`'s launch id (BLK-1's `qreg.id` would add one), so tags are the plugin's. At the
host–device boundary a handle is the ISA launch register id (`bennett_qreg`, ISA §10), which the
launch binds to a `qreg` parameter or result (§6.6). Neither value may influence a result other
than through §6.3; the rules below make them unobservable. A handle
denotes a register of n wires, n the `qalloc` operand; wire i of handle h is written h.i.

### 6.2 Provenance (maintainer decision 2)

The **provenance** of a value is the set of `ROOT` atoms of its abstract value (§5). Provenance
flows through **bitwise copies** only: `ptrtoint`/`inttoptr` at width pw, integer loads and
stores of width pw, vector lanes (including dynamic lanes, PT10: a dynamically indexed vector of
handles is a handle table), aggregate fields, memory copies, `phi`, `select`, `freeze`, call
arguments and returns (PT3–PT10). A handle-derived value (any lane or field) MAY be:

1. a handle operand of a marker (§3.2);
2. copied by the rules above, including storage in any slot the analysis tracks (§6.4);
3. an operand of `icmp eq` or `icmp ne` (§6.3);
4. an argument of a direct or indirect call of a defined function, or a return value.

Every other use is `BD-E-HANDLE-USE` (maintainer decision 2: "any other use of a
handle-derived integer is rejected fail-loud"; NS §3.1 "never dereferenced"): in particular
arithmetic, bitwise operations, `icmp` with any other predicate, any cast other than the copies
above (`trunc`, `zext`, `sext`, `bitcast` to a non-pointer-width type, `ptrtoint` to a width ≠
pw), `getelementptr` with a handle as base or index, `switch` on it, use as the pointer operand of
- every `FN(F)` atom of AV(v), resolved by `resolve` below;
- if AV(v) has the ⊤ flag: in addition every function of the required type of §7.1 (for
  `oracle`/`inplace`: every function) whose address is used anywhere in the linked program,
  resolved (decision 3 read literally: "every function whose address can reach a region marker";
  audit B-33). The set is therefore always bounded by the program.

`resolve(F)` follows aliases to their aliasee and **forwarding thunks** to their target: F is a
forwarding thunk of G iff F's body is one block holding exactly a call of G (optionally
`tail`/`musttail`/`notail`, default calling convention, no operand bundles) whose arguments are
F's parameters in order and whose function type equals F's, followed by `ret void` or `ret` of
that call's result; then `resolve(F) = resolve(G)`, except that a thunk on a cycle of thunks
resolves to itself (it is an ordinary defined function whose execution recurses until a budget or
the host stops it; 0.1's `BD-E-REGION-THUNK` is deleted).

**N0 and resolution order.** Before testing the forwarding-thunk predicate or freezing candidates, the plugin MUST apply the N0 passes of §12.2 to the virtual device bodies used by S2; §7.2 resolution then uses those bodies, and §7.3 hashes their materialised closures.

The plugin MUST retain A(S) for every original source function or alias whose address reaches the region operand, dispatching that address to its post-N0 resolved candidate even when N0 removes the forwarding call; a declaration remaining after N0 is still F(8), but an intrinsic call removed by N0 MUST NOT turn its containing defined body into an external candidate.

**Dispatch.** The region operand is classical data. The plugin compiles a body for every
candidate. At run time the operand's value is compared, by the compare tree of §14.2 over the
sorted keys, with A(S) of every **source** S (every function or alias that resolves into the
candidate set and whose address reaches v, plus every candidate itself); on equality the region
`resolve(S)` is entered. No match is the run-time trap `T-REGION` (fault code 4). A candidate
with no definition (an external declaration, a function without bitcode) is entered as a
trapped call: F(8) with a coverage line (§4.5 item 4).

**Ill-formed candidates.** A candidate that is not well-formed for the marker's kind (a type
other than §7.1's, or a violation of §7.5) is compiled as a **trap body** (`T-REGION-INVALID`,
fault code 4) with a coverage line: it may never be dispatched, so it is not a compile-time
error. It is rejected (`BD-E-REGION-TYPE`, `BD-E-REGION-MEASURE`, `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE`)
only when it is the **unique** candidate, because then every execution of the marker fails
(outline E14).

0.1's `BD-E-REGION-UNBOUNDED` and `BD-E-REGION-EXTERNAL` are deleted.

Region operands loaded from tracked memory or relative tables MUST use the reference candidate analysis and its type-compatible address-taken fallback, with no blanket TOP rejection. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-7.2, DLT-12.8-P10, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2, ISA-9.2]

### 7.3 Region identity (maintainer decision 1)

A region is identified by its content, never by its symbol. Candidates that resolve to the same
function are one region. The semantic identity of an **oracle region** in a shape (§8.4) is the
interface hash (ISA §9.2) of `@ref` in its reference object (§16.2): every lproc, data image and
layout leaf of the closure is part of it, and `@ref`, `@L<n>`, `@G<n>` are fixed names, so two
functions with equal materialised bodies have equal identity whatever their source names. Symbol
names, linkage, aliases and thunks are non-normative: diagnostics and coverage entries MAY print
them. Budget lookup uses the separate provisional `selection_identity` and the
once-only staging of §9.2, never a fixed point in this final semantic identity.

The **region key** of any region is the SHA-256 of the text printed by the LLVM printer of the
major in use for a module that contains the materialised region function and its closure, after
`strip` (local names), with the functions renamed `f0, f1, …` in depth-first preorder from the
region along direct calls in instruction order, and without datalayout, triple and module
metadata. It keys the budget table (§9.2) and is recorded in the certificate and coverage
entries. It depends on the LLVM major, which the certificate records (§24, D13).

### 7.4 Kinds by content

NS §4.2: "the plugin classifies each outlined region by its contents and the verifier checks the
declared interface". After the TDL partition (§10):

- an action region whose generated events are all permutation events (X-family gates, `qload`,
  `qstore`, `qexch`, oracle calls, permutation actions) is emitted as an ISA `perm_action`
  (ISA §5.3), which certificates can verify by T3; otherwise as an `action`;
- an oracle region stays an oracle;
- a `reversible` marker call reached from an action region's generation or from an L closure is
  not an R entry: its operand is treated as a direct call of the region function, partitioned by
  TDL inside actions and lifted inside oracles, with the call's budget record governing its
  slices (§9.3; audit B-37). 0.1's `BD-E-REGION-TIER` and `T-TIER` are deleted.

A reversible marker reached during action generation MUST retain direct-body generation and inherited TDL/effect semantics rather than creating an R entry. [deps: DLT-7.4, DLT-10, DLT-11.5-P2, ISA-3.1, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.3]

outputs; it still executes once and delivers its fault through §8.6's synthetic variant.
Every register named by the descriptor MUST be live and pairwise distinct (`T-DEAD`, `T-ALIAS`).
A width mismatch in the table above is `T-SIZE`.

### 8.3 Device bindings

`dev[j]` binds a device model for the lifted (coherent) tier (§11.3):

| Model | Code | Register | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ENTROPY` | 1 | q with n(q) = 8·2^A | an `in` region `e0:region[A,8]` holding the entropy tape, possibly superposed (NS §8 rung 8: "the entropy device is replaced by a superposed register") |
| `BLOCK` | 2 | q with n(q) = 8·512·2^A′ | an `in` region `k0` holding the disk image of 2^A′ sectors; writes go to a lifted copy-on-write overlay in the oracle's heap |
| `CLOCK` | 3 | none | the clock model's initial value is `value` |
| `CONSOLE` | 4 | q with n(q) = 8·L, or null | the first L bytes the oracle writes to the console are XORed into q as an output (payload, §8.6); with a null q console bytes are discarded with the oracle's other writes |

At most one binding per model. A model without a binding is **unbound**: an effect on it in L
code faults with code 8 on the executions that reach it (§11.3; NS §4.2).

### 8.4 Shapes and dispatch

A **shape** is the tuple (region candidate F; for each parameter its kind, mode, register width
n(q) and `size`; n(tgt) or "none"; for each device binding its model and n(q)). The kind, mode,
model, `size` and `value` fields have value sets (§5.3); register widths come from the register
file (§6.5), whose roots have known widths. The plugin compiles one reference object (§16.2) per
shape that the value sets allow, taking every unbounded kind or mode field over its finite
domain (5 kinds, 2 modes, 4 models), so the number of shapes is always bounded. At run time the
caller's tier reads the descriptor and selects the compiled shape with the compare tree of
§14.2; a descriptor that matches no compiled shape traps (`T-IFACE`), which happens only for a
field outside its table. `value` and `size` of `ARG` bindings are the oracle's classical
arguments and are not part of the shape. *(non-normative)* With one valid value per shape field there is one shape per
candidate. Initial zero possibilities still participate in §5; kind 0 is outside the
five-kind table and takes T-IFACE rather than creating an additional valid shape.

The marker then issues, in the caller's tier, one **oracle invocation** (§8.6) whose operands are
the static registers the descriptor's handles denote (§6.5), the budget token (§9.5) and the
classical arguments.

ARG bindings MUST carry the caller's fixed-at-entry values in parameter order without specialising their values into the shape, and pointer arguments MUST retain entry-state semantics. [deps: DLT-8.2-P3, DLT-8.4-P1, DLT-8.5, ISA-4.7-P1, ISA-12.3-P2]

### 8.5 Entry state

**Reference semantics.** The region function runs on a **private copy of the caller's whole
classical memory at the call**, with the bound inputs; its writes are discarded except through
`OUT`, `tgt` and the bound device outputs (NS §4.2: "every other write being restored
workspace"). Constant globals, and mutable globals that only the oracle's own L closure writes,
equal their initialisers at every call.

**Realisation on ISA 0.8.** Let R be the abstract objects the oracle closure may load (§5.4),
minus constant globals and mutable globals written by no function outside the L closure.
Translate source read-set objects after normalisation to runtime backing allocations. If
R includes a caller alloca/frame, include its live shadow-stack backing storage and metadata,
bytes and addresses. If reachability is uncertain, include all live caller heap/stack backing
allocations. Whenever the L closure uses the shadow stack, add its state globals to G and
its block to Hb with current base, size and stack pointer, even without a source heap object.
Include the logical frame/allocation table of §17.3, dynamic extents, liveness and
save/restore state in G whenever stack access or stack operations can execute. Replay
these bytes before private allocations/accesses and let ss_init adopt them; a live
backing entry alone does not recreate live program frames. All such metadata mutations
invalidate snapshot reuse independently of the byte read set.
Allocator metadata is an independent entry-state dependency: allocation, deallocation,
allocation-validity checks and observation of allocation-derived addresses read it even
without an LLVM load. Replay the complete caller allocation history (sizes and live bits)
whenever any can execute, including when the byte read set R is empty. Bytes are included
only for byte dependencies; the sound default is the complete table, with a smaller proven
dependency optional. Only when both byte and allocator-state dependencies are absent may
transfer be omitted and globals start at their images
(ISA §3.7), as in rung 4 route A (SQLite §5.2(i)). After replay heap_used, atop and stack-state
values equal the caller's call-entry values.
Otherwise the plugin emits an **entry-state transfer**:

1. **Snapshot.** At the call the caller's tier forms the byte string
   S = k ‖ (size_i ‖ live_i)_{i<k} ‖ G ‖ Hb, where k is an 8-byte little-endian count of heap allocations in the
   caller's allocation table (in allocation order, ISA §7.1), `size_i` and `live_i` are 8-byte
   little-endian fields (`live_i` is 0 or 1), G is the bytes of the mutable globals in R in `layout.globals` order,
   and Hb is the bytes of every live heap allocation in allocation order if R contains a heap
   object or shadow-stack backing allocation (else empty); each live Hb segment has exactly size_i bytes, even for size_i=0. S is zero-padded to 2^A_s bytes, where A_s is the smallest exponent with
   2^A_s ≥ 8 + 16·N_alloc + Σ|G| + `heap_limit_max` (N_alloc the allocation-table capacity of
   the layout, §19.5); A_s is static.
   The snapshot bytes S are assembled in a plugin-added unnamed CC global of 2^A_s bytes,
   zero-filled before every build; thus all 16-byte leaf loads, including padding, are valid.
2. **Register.** The CC tier builds a register of 8·2^A_s wires holding S with a **concat
   tree** of A_s − 3 static-width CC functions `B_j` (j = 4, …, A_s): `B_4(p)` executes
   `qreg.new any 128`, loads the 16 bytes at p as two `i64` values and makes the `oracle_call`
   of the runtime oracle `@xor128` (§16.5) that XORs them into the register (with a token minted
   at launch for budget (6, 0, 0, 0) and a fresh 4-wire fault register, which stays clean and is
   freed); `B_j(p)` (j > 4)
   returns `qreg.concat B_{j−1}(p) B_{j−1}(p + 2^{j−1})`. Every type is static and the cost is
   2^(A_s−4) region calls. The register is bound as an extra `in` region `s0:region[A_s,8]`.
3. **Replay.** The wrapper prologue (§16.2) calls the runtimes function
   `__bennett_rt_entry_replay(s0)`, which for i < k executes `__bennett_nz_alloc(size_i)` (ISA
   `lift.alloc`) and, if `live_i` = 0, `__bennett_nz_free` of the result. ISA §4.2 `lift.alloc`
   and ISA §7.1 `alloc` share the rule "bump + max(n, 1) from `heap_base`", so the replay
   reproduces the caller's heap addresses exactly (audit O-07). It then copies G into the
   globals (by the driver-generated table `__bennett_rt_entry_table` of their addresses and
   sizes) and Hb into the replayed live allocations.
4. **Release.** The snapshot register holds the basis state |S⟩ and is unentangled after every

## 14. Calls and control transfer

### 14.1 Direct calls and recursion

Direct calls lower to CC/R `call` (ISA §4.1) and to lifted `lift.call`/`lift.ret` lprocs (ISA
§3.7), with the depth and stack faults of the budget (§9). Recursion is unrestricted.

### 14.2 Indirect calls

- **Candidate set** of an indirect call with callee value v: every `FN(F)` atom of AV(v) (§5),
  resolved through aliases; if AV(v) has the ⊤ flag, in addition every function whose LLVM
  function type equals the call's and whose address is used anywhere in the linked program
  (sound for defined C, C++ and Rust: a call through an incompatible type is undefined, and
  compatible C types lower to the same LLVM type under opaque pointers; SQLite §3). The device
  closure includes every candidate (§4.1).
- **Dispatch**: the compare tree of §16.4 over the candidates' A(F) values, sorted unsigned, with
  a static call at each leaf: CC/R `icmp eq` + `br` + `call @F_k` (ISA §4.1), lifted `lift.icmp.eq`
  + `qbranch` + `lift.call @L(F_k)` (ISA §4.2). `qjumpr` is intra-function only (ISA §4.2) and is
  not used for calls.
- **No match** (a value that is the address of no function of the program, or of a function
  without bitcode): F(4) `FAULT_UB_TRAP` (outline E13; calling through such a pointer is undefined
  or reaches code without bitcode, which the coverage report names). **Zero candidates**: F(4) at
  the site.
- **Trampolines** (`llvm.init.trampoline(t, f, nval)`, `llvm.adjust.trampoline(t)`): `init`
  stores the tag `0x42454E54` (i32), A(f) and nval in the trampoline memory t (runtimes
  `__bennett_rt_tramp_init`); `adjust` returns t. Before the compare tree, an indirect call whose
  callee value v points to a live allocation holding the tag at offset 0 calls the stored
  function with the stored nest value as its `nest` argument.
- Calls through a variadic function type use §14.3. The same compare-tree construction serves
  region operands (§7.2), handle ids (§6.5), descriptor shapes (§8.4), `switch` (§14.8) and
  `indirectbr` (§14.9).

```llvm example=indirect-table verdict=accept callees=gt,lt
define internal i32 @lt(i32 %a, i32 %b) {
  %c = icmp slt i32 %a, %b
  %r = zext i1 %c to i32
  ret i32 %r
}

define internal i32 @gt(i32 %a, i32 %b) {
  %c = icmp sgt i32 %a, %b
  %r = zext i1 %c to i32
  ret i32 %r
}

@cmps = internal constant [2 x ptr] [ptr @lt, ptr @gt]

define void @pick(i32 %which, i32 %a, i32 %b) {
entry:
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  %k = and i32 %which, 1
  %i = zext i32 %k to i64
  %p = getelementptr [2 x ptr], ptr @cmps, i64 0, i64 %i
  %f = load ptr, ptr %p
  %r = call i32 %f(i32 %a, i32 %b)
`T-OP`, `T-CLASS`, `T-IFACE`, `T-BUDGET`, `T-TAINT-HOST`. Deleted: `T-BORROW` (outside generations
`qborrow` is a clean allocation, bd `.9`), `T-TIER` (§7.4).

### 22.4 Warnings

`BD-W-QSTORE-UNCLEAN` (§6.7), `BD-W-TAINT-HOST` (TDL-11). A
warning never changes a verdict.

## 23. Versioning

- The **full version string** of this document (`0.2.12`) is the unit of compatibility: a plugin
  implements exactly one dialect version and targets exactly one ISA version (§19.9); drafts are
  not compatible with each other. [deps: DLT-23, DLT-19.9]
- The consumer-gap dispositions revise the full dialect to `0.2.12`. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-12.2-P11, DLT-23]
  The lifting map remains `0.2.9` (global-numbering correction); `norm`, `handles` and
  `regions` remain `0.2.8`; unchanged oracle-abi, tdl and effects remain `0.2.4`, marker/header,
  asm and corpus remain `0.2`, platform and driver remain `0.2.1`. Every reference object is checked
  again; unchanged ISA-0.8 interface signatures retain their hashes. The map version
  is recorded in certificate metadata, not inserted into ISA-owned interface signatures.
- Every semantic change bumps the version; the reference objects and hashes of §16 depend on the
  ISA version string, the map and the layout, and change with any of them (0.1's hashes changed
  with the wrapper form of §16.2).
- The build record (§19.9) records the runtimes, arith and asm-table hashes and the budget-table
  hash; `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml` records `dialect` and the targeted `isa`; the change rules are in
  `spec/README.md`.

## 24. Deferred mechanics (with defaults)

Only mechanics appear here. Each row states the capability it keeps; none has a default of
reject, refuse, fault or no effect for something the north star promises.

| Id | Mechanics | Default | Capability kept |
|---|---|---|---|
| D12 | host-stack addresses passed into device code (NS §3.2 "where possible") | executions they influence are layout-dependent (§18.7) and validated against the reference interpreter | every program runs; only the comparison oracle changes |
| D13 | a region key stable across LLVM majors (§7.3) | the key depends on the major, which the certificate records | identity within a link is by content |
| D15 | the comparison-link bases (§19.5) | `null_size` 4096, data at 4096, text at 0x400000 or above the device ranges, options to change them | native-equal addresses |
| D20 | single passes outside the envelope (§21.1) | unsupported unless listed (they are LLVM bugs, spike findings §2) | every envelope pipeline |
| D22 | output file names and diagnostic text | plugin-defined; the codes of §22 are normative | – |
| D23 | ThinLTO with the plugin | full LTO: one merged module | every program merges into one module (audit D23, M) |
| D24 | multi-threaded device code (NS §9.11) | the single-thread assumption (N9), recorded in the certificate | uniprocessor programs, rung 6's configurations |
| D26 | the binary layout of descriptors and budget records beyond the C view | the LLVM struct layout under the module's datalayout (§8.1, §9.1) | every target |
| D27 | budget defaults (§9.2) | 1048576 steps, 1048576 heap bytes, 65536 stack cells, depth 256, `sstack` 65536 | every budget is overridable |
| D28 | the in-place exhaustive width (§8.7) | 24 bits; sampled evidence and the `T-CLEAN` net above | every pair is verified, as proof or evidence |
| D29 | the static register-file size for unbounded sites (§6.5) | 64 instances, then the BLK-1 blocker trap | – (the case itself is a blocker) |
| D30 | further device models (ISA §17 item 3) | the four of ISA §7.3; another device's effect is `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED` until the ISA adds it | the four modelled devices in every tier |
| D31 | target ABI rows for `va_list` (§14.3), asm (§15) and target intrinsics beyond x86-64, AArch64 and RISC-V64 | added with the target | the three targets of the rungs |
| D32 | the platform row lists and board constants (§20) | the patterns of §20.2–§20.6 until the §20.7 spike | rungs 6 and 7 |
| D33 | `vscale` (§12.5) and the declared CPU model for `cpuid` (§15.2) | `vscale_range` minimum; the `cpuid` leaves of the build machine, recorded in the build record | scalable-vector and CPU-feature code |

## 25. Blockers and ISA items

### 25.1 Blockers

A **blocker** is a capability the dialect fully specifies whose stated case has no realisation on
ISA 0.8. BLK-3 has been retired by §8.7. Unlike the original outline list,
BLK-5 can affect typed fault points in R-tier rungs; successful executions remain covered.
The maintainer ruled
(2026-10-29) that ISA 0.9 waits until this dialect is council-accepted.

| Id | Code | NS capability | Affected case | ISA 0.8 realisation that exists | Exact ISA change |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BLK-1** = ISA-4 | `BD-B-ISA4` | NS §3.1 handle tables; §4.1, §4.2 dynamic allocation ("real allocation"); Bennett.jl vectors of qubits; adjoints of generations whose register set is known only at run time | allocation sites or entry parameter/result roots whose handles persist across entries without a finite instance bound (§6.5); register widths with value set ⊤ (§3.4); a Co width with an unbounded value set (TDL-10) | direct `qreg` values for handles that stay out of memory; static register files with dispatch for bounded sites (§6.5) | (i) a dynamic-width type `qreg[*]`; `q = qreg.new cls n` with n an `i64` value yields `qreg[*]`; `w = qreg.width q`. (ii) Binding a `qreg[*]` to a `wire[n]` or `region[A,W]` slot checks the width at the call: CC trap, R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`, on a mismatch. (iii) `id = qreg.id q` (non-consuming; the §10 launch id) and `q = qreg.from_id id` (yields `qreg[*]`; a trap if the id is not live, is viewed or is consumed). History frames: `qreg.id` is pure; `qreg.from_id` records the handle reference |
| **BLK-2** = ISA-1 | `BD-B-ISA1` | NS §4.2 "run-time budgets come from the host or from measurement results" | budget fields computed on the device (§9.5 case 3) | pre-minted finite sets; stub-minted copies of launch arguments (§9.5) | a CC instruction `tk = budget.mint @region s h st d` (`i64` operands) with the ISA §5.4 validation of `bennett_budget` as a CC trap; the token is scoped to the run; an impure history frame recording the token |
| **BLK-5** = ISA-7 | `BD-B-ISA7` | NS §4.2 named fault outcomes and R reversible prefixes | every R entry (§11.5), including mandatory budget codes 1/2/3 and explicit __bennett_nz_fault(C); direct calls under §7.4 excluded | CC hostcall reports C; generic R trap stops execution but does not satisfy typed reporting | R `fault C`: terminate with fault record code C, no later source effect, retain reversible prefix history; specify the implicit trap-cause-to-code table (div0 5, overflow 6, memory 7, trap 4, allocation failure 2) |
| **BLK-4** = new ISA-12 (audit B-38) | `BD-B-ISA12` | NS §4.2 quantum allocation inside reversible-tier code | `qalloc`/`qborrow`/`qfree` in an R closure with no finite instance bound (§7.5b); entry-state transfer from R code (§8.5) | hoisting to the CC caller with a static count (§7.5b) | permit `qreg.new`, `qreg.free`, `qreg.slice` and `qreg.concat` in R; the R history records them; the reversal of `qreg.new` is `qreg.free` with the cleanliness obligation (simulator check; hardware per ISA §17 item 14); the reversal of `qreg.free` is `qreg.new` (clean); `measure` stays CC-only |

BLK-5's checked witnesses are `r-fault-code` (explicit code 2) and
`r-budget-prefix` (the pre-instruction code 1 boundary). Their verdict is
`blocked:BD-B-ISA7` when grading against ISA 0.8. The exact change is now
specified in the ISA 0.9 draft, commit `61550b4` (section 0.2 K5 and section
4.1 `fault C`); its presence in that draft does not change this draft's ISA target.

On ISA 0.8 a module has verdict blocked:BD-B-ISA7 if its device program contains
an R-tier variant reached as an R entry: a reversible region candidate invoked outside
generation, or the §19.2 whole-program R entry. Every such variant has the §17.6 budget
check, regardless of supplied run-time limits. A reversible call treated as a direct call
    }
    std::sort(flagged.begin(),flagged.end());
    kept.insert(kept.end(),flagged.begin(),flagged.end());
    kept.insert(kept.end(),firstInt,ids.end());
    ids=std::move(kept);
    firstStar=std::lower_bound(ids.begin(),ids.end(),StarBit);
    firstInt=std::lower_bound(firstStar,ids.end(),IntBit);
  }
  // A chain of displaced addresses (a pointer recurrence through memory, or a copy between
  // offsets of one object) denotes PT2's modular orbit, which only ends after 2^60 or so
  // elements. Take the orbit, the residue class of the differences' lowest set bit with PT2's
  // unknown-address alternative, past K offsets of one provenance: the bound §5.1 sets on an
  // index set, so an index domain within K keeps its exact offsets. §5.1 states K for INT atoms
  // only; b-dialect-spec-5u3 asks it to state the offset bound too.
  // Only under -bennett-analysis-early-chain-close (not the reference): also past 4 offsets
  // outside the object, and past 32 of an object of no literal extent.
  constexpr size_t OffsetChain=4096,EarlyLeftObject=4,EarlyNoExtent=32;
  const size_t LeftObject=EarlyChainClose?EarlyLeftObject:SIZE_MAX,NoExtent=EarlyChainClose?EarlyNoExtent:SIZE_MAX;
  size_t pointers=firstStar-ids.begin();
  bool chained=false;
  if(closeChains && pointers>std::min<size_t>(OffsetChain,LeftObject)) {
    struct Tally {uint32_t total=0,outside=0;uint64_t extent=0;bool known=false;};
    llvm::DenseMap<const Value *,Tally> counts;
    for(auto it=ids.begin();it!=firstStar && !chained;++it) {
      const Atom &a=table.atoms[*it&IndexMask];
      if(a.kind!=Atom::Ptr)continue;
      auto &t=counts[a.object];
      if(!t.known) {t.known=true;t.extent=objectExtent(a.object);}
      ++t.total;
      if((uint64_t)a.offset>t.extent)++t.outside; // One past the end is an ordinary address.
      chained=t.total>OffsetChain || t.outside>LeftObject || (t.extent==UINT64_MAX && t.total>NoExtent) || collapsedObjects().count(a.object);
    }
  }
  if(chained) {
    struct Provenance {const Value *object;bool numerical;bool operator<(const Provenance &o) const {
      return object!=o.object?std::less<const Value *>{}(object,o.object):numerical<o.numerical;}};
    std::map<Provenance,std::vector<uint32_t>> parts;
    for(auto it=ids.begin();it!=firstStar;++it) {
      const Atom &a=table.atoms[*it&IndexMask];
      if(a.kind==Atom::Ptr)parts[{a.object,a.numerical}].push_back(*it);
    }
    std::vector<uint32_t> doomed,added;
    for(auto &part:parts) {
      uint64_t extent=objectExtent(part.first.object),outside=0;
      for(uint32_t id:part.second)outside+=(uint64_t)table.atoms[id&IndexMask].offset>extent;
      bool collapsed=collapsedObjects().count(part.first.object);
      if(!collapsed && part.second.size()<=OffsetChain && outside<=LeftObject && !(extent==UINT64_MAX && part.second.size()>NoExtent))continue;
      uint64_t low=0;const Atom &first=table.atoms[part.second[0]&IndexMask];
      for(uint32_t id:part.second) {
        const Atom &a=table.atoms[id&IndexMask];
        if(a.stride>0)low|=(uint64_t)a.stride;
        low|=(uint64_t)a.offset-(uint64_t)first.offset;
      }
      Atom closed=first;
      unsigned zeros=low?llvm::countr_zero(low):63;
      if(zeros==0 || collapsed) {closed.offset=0;closed.stride=-1;}
      else {closed.stride=int64_t(1)<<zeros;closed.offset=(int64_t)((uint64_t)first.offset&((uint64_t)closed.stride-1));}
      doomed.insert(doomed.end(),part.second.begin(),part.second.end());
      added.push_back(table.intern(closed));
    }
    if(!added.empty()) {
      Atom unknown{Atom::Ptr,nullptr,0,-1,APInt(1,0)};unknown.numerical=true;
      added.push_back(table.intern(unknown));
      top=true;
      std::sort(doomed.begin(),doomed.end());
      std::vector<uint32_t> kept;
      for(uint32_t id:ids)if(!std::binary_search(doomed.begin(),doomed.end(),id))kept.push_back(id);
      kept.insert(kept.end(),added.begin(),added.end());
      std::sort(kept.begin(),kept.end(),idLess);
      kept.erase(std::unique(kept.begin(),kept.end()),kept.end());
      ids=std::move(kept);
      // A class of step 2^k may itself be (X,*) now, which absorbs the object's rest.
      canonical(ids,top);
      return;
    }
  }
  if(!top && (size_t)(ids.end()-firstInt)>4096)top=true;
  if(top)ids.erase(firstInt,ids.end());
}
struct UnionKey {
  uint64_t a,b;unsigned top; // Bit 1: chains closed.
}
void Analysis::store(const AV &v, const AV &p, uint64_t size, bool filled) {
  for (auto atomAt : p.atoms)
    if (atomAt.kind == Atom::Ptr) {
      seedOnDemand(atomAt.object);
      // Only under -bennett-analysis-early-chain-close (not the reference): past as many written
      // slots as its extent could hold distinct 4-byte fields (64 with no known extent), collapse
      // an object to (X,*), so that a later load overlaps that slot and PT6 gives top.
      if(atomAt.object && collapsedObjects().count(atomAt.object)) {atomAt.offset=0;atomAt.stride=-1;}
      // (X,*) denotes one location set whatever offset spelled it (§5.1).
      if(atomAt.stride==-1)atomAt.offset=0;
      const Atom &a=atomAt;
      // Initial zero images with the same start are nested intervals. Their
      // exact union is the largest extent, not a new slot for every loop round.
      if(filled && !v.top && v.atoms.size()==1 && v.atoms.begin()->kind==Atom::Int && v.atoms.begin()->integer.isZero()) {
        bool found=false;
        for(size_t n:otherSlots[a.object]) {
          auto &s=slots[n];
          if(s.filled && s.offset==a.offset && s.stride==a.stride && s.numerical==a.numerical && !s.value.top && s.value.atoms.size()==1 && s.value.atoms.begin()->kind==Atom::Int && s.value.atoms.begin()->integer.isZero()) {
            if(size>s.bytes) {
              slotIndex.erase(SlotKey{s.object,s.offset,s.stride,s.bytes,s.filled,s.numerical});
              s.bytes=size;slotIndex.emplace(SlotKey{s.object,s.offset,s.stride,s.bytes,s.filled,s.numerical},n);slotChanged(n,false);
            }
            found=true;break;
          }
        }
        if(found)continue;
      }
      SlotKey key{a.object,a.offset,a.stride,size,filled,a.numerical};
      auto [it,inserted]=slotIndex.emplace(key,slots.size());
      if(inserted) {
        slots.push_back({a.object,a.offset,a.stride,size,v,filled,a.numerical});
        indexSlot(it->second);
        slotChanged(it->second);
        if(EarlyChainClose && a.object && !filled && a.stride!=-1 && !collapsedObjects().count(a.object)) {
          uint64_t extent=objectExtent(a.object);
          uint64_t limit=extent==UINT64_MAX?64:std::max<uint64_t>(64,extent/4);
          if(++writtenSlots[a.object]>limit) {
            collapsedObjects().insert(a.object);
            unionMemo().clear();displaceMemo.clear(); // Their results predate the collapse.
          }
        }
      } else if(slots[it->second].value.merge(v)) {noteSlotEdges(it->second);slotChanged(it->second);}
    }
}
AV Analysis::read(Value *base, uint64_t offset, Type *t) {
  AV p=get(base), shifted;shifted.top=p.top;
  for(auto x:p.atoms) {if(x.kind==Atom::Ptr)x.offset+=offset;shifted.atoms.insert(x);}
  return load(shifted,t);
}
// A local straight-line reaching store is an independent proof of the actual
// descriptor field at this call. It does not change the normative S2 solution.
AV Analysis::readAt(CallBase &call, Value *base, uint64_t offset, Type *type) {
  auto address=get(base);
  if(address.top || address.atoms.size()!=1) return read(base,offset,type);
  auto p=*address.atoms.begin();
  if(p.kind!=Atom::Ptr || p.stride || !isa<AllocaInst>(p.object)) return read(base,offset,type);
  p.offset+=offset;
  uint64_t bytes=M.getDataLayout().getTypeStoreSize(type).getKnownMinValue();
  for(auto it=call.getIterator();it!=call.getParent()->begin();) {

 A **shape** is the tuple (region candidate F; for each parameter its kind, mode, register width
 n(q) and `size`; n(tgt) or "none"; for each device binding its model and n(q)). The kind, mode,
-model, `size` and `value` fields have value sets (§5.3); register widths come from the register
-file (§6.5), whose roots have known widths. The plugin compiles one reference object (§16.2) per
+model, `size` and `value` fields have value sets (§5.3); register widths come from the register's
+realisation (§6.5): known for a root with a finite width set, else the run-time `qreg.width`. The plugin compiles one reference object (§16.2) per
 shape that the value sets allow, taking every unbounded kind or mode field over its finite
 domain (5 kinds, 2 modes, 4 models), so the number of shapes is always bounded. At run time the
 caller's tier reads the descriptor and selects the compiled shape with the compare tree of
 §14.2; a descriptor that matches no compiled shape traps (`T-IFACE`), which happens only for a
-field outside its table. `value` and `size` of `ARG` bindings are the oracle's classical
+field outside its table. When an interface width or descriptor extent is not finitely enumerated by AV, the reference dispatch domain MUST include every valid representable shape allowed by the marker's unsigned i64 fields and the existing parameter-binding checks, rather than treating AV top as a runtime interface failure. ## 14. Calls and control transfer

### 14.1 Direct calls and recursion

Direct calls lower to CC/R `call` (ISA §4.1) and to lifted `lift.call`/`lift.ret` lprocs (ISA
§3.7), with the depth and stack faults of the budget (§9). Recursion is unrestricted.

### 14.2 Indirect calls

- **Candidate set** of an indirect call with callee value v: every `FN(F)` atom of AV(v) (§5),
  resolved through aliases; if AV(v) has the ⊤ flag, in addition every function whose LLVM
  function type equals the call's and whose address is used anywhere in the linked program
  (sound for defined C, C++ and Rust: a call through an incompatible type is undefined, and
  compatible C types lower to the same LLVM type under opaque pointers; SQLite §3). The device
  closure includes every candidate (§4.1).
- **Dispatch**: the compare tree of §16.4 over the candidates' A(F) values, sorted unsigned, with
  a static call at each leaf: CC/R `icmp eq` + `br` + `call @F_k` (ISA §4.1), lifted `lift.icmp.eq`
  + `qbranch` + `lift.call @L(F_k)` (ISA §4.2). `qjumpr` is intra-function only (ISA §4.2) and is
  not used for calls.
- **No match** (a value that is the address of no function of the program, or of a function
  without bitcode): F(4) `FAULT_UB_TRAP` (outline E13; calling through such a pointer is undefined
  or reaches code without bitcode, which the coverage report names). **Zero candidates**: F(4) at
  the site.
- **Trampolines** (`llvm.init.trampoline(t, f, nval)`, `llvm.adjust.trampoline(t)`): `init`
  stores the tag `0x42454E54` (i32), A(f) and nval in the trampoline memory t (runtimes
## 23. Versioning

- The **full version string** of this document (`0.2.12`) is the unit of compatibility: a plugin
  implements exactly one dialect version and targets exactly one ISA version (§19.9); drafts are
  not compatible with each other. [deps: DLT-23, DLT-19.9]
- The consumer-gap dispositions revise the full dialect to `0.2.12`. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-12.2-P11, DLT-23]
  The lifting map remains `0.2.9` (global-numbering correction); `norm`, `handles` and
  `regions` remain `0.2.8`; unchanged oracle-abi, tdl and effects remain `0.2.4`, marker/header,
  asm and corpus remain `0.2`, platform and driver remain `0.2.1`. Every reference object is checked
  again; unchanged ISA-0.8 interface signatures retain their hashes. The map version
  is recorded in certificate metadata, not inserted into ISA-owned interface signatures.
- Every semantic change bumps the version; the reference objects and hashes of §16 depend on the
  ISA version string, the map and the layout, and change with any of them (0.1's hashes changed
  with the wrapper form of §16.2).
- The build record (§19.9) records the runtimes, arith and asm-table hashes and the budget-table
  hash; `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml` records `dialect` and the targeted `isa`; the change rules are in
  `spec/README.md`.

## 24. Deferred mechanics (with defaults)

Only mechanics appear here. Each row states the capability it keeps; none has a default of
reject, refuse, fault or no effect for something the north star promises.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""§5.1's K is the only bound of the reference analysis (advisor ruling on b-dialect-spec-5u3).

Offsets are never closed before K offsets of one provenance, and no object collapses for its slot
count, unless the non-reference flag -bennett-analysis-early-chain-close asks for it.
"""
import argparse,json,subprocess,sys
from contracts import ROOT as R
def chain(count,offset):
 """A select chain over `count` GEPs of %b, so that %p<count-1> holds them all."""
 lines=[f' %g0 = getelementptr i8, ptr %b, i64 {offset(0)}']
 prev='%g0'
 for n in range(1,count):
  lines.append(f' %g{n} = getelementptr i8, ptr %b, i64 {offset(n)}')
  lines.append(f' %p{n} = select i1 %c, ptr {prev}, ptr %g{n}')
  prev=f'%p{n}'
 return '\n'.join(lines),prev
def unknown_extent(count):
 body,last=chain(count,lambda n:n*8)
 return f'''declare ptr @malloc(i64)
define ptr @top(i1 %c, i64 %n) {{
 %b = call ptr @malloc(i64 %n)
{body}
 ret ptr {last}
}}''',last
def past_extent(count):
 body,last=chain(count,lambda n:1000+n*8)
 return f'''@g = internal global [2 x i64] zeroinitializer
define ptr @top(i1 %c) {{
 %b = getelementptr i8, ptr @g, i64 0
{body}
 ret ptr {last}
}}''',last
def many_slots(count):
 stores='\n'.join(f' %s{n} = getelementptr i8, ptr %b, i64 {n*8}\n store i64 {n+100}, ptr %s{n}' for n in range(count))
 return f'''declare ptr @malloc(i64)
define i64 @top(i64 %n) {{
 %b = call ptr @malloc(i64 %n)
{stores}
 %v = load i64, ptr %s0
 ret i64 %v
}}'''
INTEGER_ORBIT='''@g = internal global [8 x i64] zeroinitializer
define internal i64 @cycle() {
entry:
 %base = ptrtoint ptr @g to i64
 br label %loop
loop:
 %n = phi i64 [ %base, %entry ], [ %sum, %loop ]
 %sum = add i64 %n, 8
 br label %loop
}
define i64 @top() {
 %r = call i64 @cycle()
 ret i64 %r
}'''
UNDEFINED_REACH='''declare void @ext(ptr)
@x = internal global ptr null
@y = internal global i64 5
@z = internal global i64 5
define i64 @top() {
 call void @ext(ptr @x)
 store ptr @y, ptr @x
 %reached = load i64, ptr @y
 %apart = load i64, ptr @z
 %sum = add i64 %reached, %apart
 ret i64 %sum
}'''
CALL_CYCLE='''define i32 @walk(i32 %depth) {
entry:
 %again = icmp ne i32 %depth, 0
 br i1 %again, label %deeper, label %leaf
deeper:
 %r = call i32 @walk(i32 0)
 %next = add i32 %r, 1
 br label %leaf
leaf:
 %out = phi i32 [ %next, %deeper ], [ 0, %entry ]
 ret i32 %out
}'''
LANE_ORBIT='''define <2 x i16> @top() {
entry: br label %loop
loop:
 %v = phi <2 x i16> [ <i16 0, i16 16>, %entry ], [ %next, %loop ]
 %next = add <2 x i16> %v, <i16 16, i16 16>
 %c = extractelement <2 x i16> %next, i64 0
 %t = icmp eq i16 %c, 0
 br i1 %t, label %out, label %loop
out:
 ret <2 x i16> %v
}'''
PT5_SETS='''@g = internal global [8 x i64] zeroinitializer
@h = internal global [8 x i64] zeroinitializer
define i64 @top(i1 %c) {
 %p = select i1 %c, ptr @g, ptr @h
 %i = ptrtoint ptr %p to i64
 %n = select i1 %c, i64 8, i64 16
 %d = add i64 %i, %n
 %j = ptrtoint ptr @g to i64
 %diff = sub i64 %i, %j
 %neg = sub i64 100, %j
 %r = add i64 %d, %diff
 %s = add i64 %r, %neg
 ret i64 %s
}'''
def solve(major,name,body,early=False):
 dest=R/f'.tmp/reference-k/{major}/{name}';dest.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
 src=dest/'in.ll';src.write_text('target datalayout = "e-p:64:64"\n'+body+'\n');report=dest/'report.json'
 argv=[f'/usr/lib/llvm-{major}/bin/opt','-load-pass-plugin='+str(R/f'build/libbennett-plugin-{major}.so'),'-passes=bennett','-bennett-dialect=0.2.10','-bennett-analysis-only','-bennett-report='+str(report),'-disable-output',str(src)]
 if early:argv.insert(4,'-bennett-analysis-early-chain-close')
 x=subprocess.run(argv,capture_output=True,text=True,timeout=600)
 if x.returncode:raise AssertionError(f'{name}: {x.stderr[-800:]}')
 return json.loads(report.read_text()),x.stderr
def value(data,name,function='top'):
 found=[v for v in data['values'] if v['function']==function and v['value']==name]
 if len(found)!=1:raise AssertionError(f'{name}: {len(found)} records')
 return found[0]
def main():
 p=argparse.ArgumentParser();p.add_argument('--major',type=int,required=True);a=p.parse_args();m=a.major;results=[]
 def check(name,ok,detail=''):results.append({'case':name,'ok':bool(ok),'detail':str(detail)[:400]})
 # Reference: 40 offsets of an object of no literal extent are exact, without top.
 body,last=unknown_extent(40);data,_=solve(m,'unknown-40',body);v=value(data,last)
 check('unknown-extent-40-exact',not v['top'] and len(v['atoms'])==40 and all(x.endswith(',0)') for x in v['atoms']),v)
 # Reference: K offsets stay exact; one more takes the orbit. The boundary is §5.1's own.
 body,last=unknown_extent(4096);data,_=solve(m,'unknown-4096',body);v=value(data,last)
 check('unknown-extent-K-exact',not v['top'] and len(v['atoms'])==4096,{'top':v['top'],'atoms':len(v['atoms'])})
 body,last=unknown_extent(4097);data,_=solve(m,'unknown-4097',body);v=value(data,last)
 check('unknown-extent-K+1-closes',v['top'] and len(v['atoms'])<8,{'top':v['top'],'atoms':v['atoms'][:8]})
 # Reference: eight addresses outside the object stay explicit beside PT2's unknown address.
 body,last=past_extent(8);data,_=solve(m,'past-8',body);v=value(data,last)
 explicit=[x for x in v['atoms'] if x.startswith('PTR(@g,') and x.endswith(',0)')]
 check('past-extent-8-exact',len(explicit)==8,v)
 # Reference: 80 written slots of one object do not collapse it; a load reads its own slot.
 data,_=solve(m,'slots-80',many_slots(80));v=value(data,'%v')
 check('slots-80-no-collapse',not v['top'] and 'INT(100)' in v['atoms'] and 'INT(101)' not in v['atoms'],v)
 # An additive cycle of step 8 is exactly the class of its step: PT5 leaves no reason for (X,*).
 data,_=solve(m,'integer-orbit',INTEGER_ORBIT);v=value(data,'%n','cycle')
 check('integer-orbit-residue',v['top'] and 'PTR(@g,0,8)' in v['atoms'] and 'PTR(@g,0,-1)' not in v['atoms'],v)
 # PT12 reaches through memory, also over an edge a later step creates; an unreached object stays exact.
 data,_=solve(m,'pt12-reach',UNDEFINED_REACH)
 check('pt12-reaches-later-edge',value(data,'%reached')['top'] and not value(data,'%apart')['top'] and value(data,'%apart')['atoms']==['INT(5)'],[value(data,'%reached'),value(data,'%apart')])
 # An additive cycle through a call's return is solved directly; a vector lane's orbit within K is whole and exact.
 data,_=solve(m,'call-cycle',CALL_CYCLE);v=value(data,'%r','walk')
 check('call-cycle-top',v['top'] and data['analysis_rounds']<100,{'rounds':data['analysis_rounds'],'v':v})
 data,_=solve(m,'lane-orbit',LANE_ORBIT);v=value(data,'%v','top')
 check('lane-orbit-exact',data['analysis_rounds']<100 and not v['top'],{'rounds':data['analysis_rounds'],'top':v['top']})
 # PT5 on address sets: one displaced image per INT, a conservative one for address minus address and INT minus address.
 data,_=solve(m,'pt5-sets',PT5_SETS)
 v=value(data,'%d');check('pt5-displaced-exact',set(v['atoms'])=={'PTR(@g,8,0)','PTR(@g,16,0)','PTR(@h,8,0)','PTR(@h,16,0)'},v)
 v=value(data,'%diff');check('pt5-address-difference',v['top'] and 'PTR(@g,0,-1)' in v['atoms'] and 'PTR(@h,0,-1)' in v['atoms'],v)
 v=value(data,'%neg');check('pt5-int-minus-address',v['top'] and 'PTR(@g,0,-1)' in v['atoms'],v)
 # The flag is the only way to the earlier closure, and it says so.
 body,last=unknown_extent(40);data,err=solve(m,'early-unknown-40',body,early=True);v=value(data,last)
 check('flag-closes-early',v['top'] and len(v['atoms'])<8,{'top':v['top'],'atoms':v['atoms'][:8]})
 check('flag-labelled','NOT the reference analysis' in err,err[:200])
 data,_=solve(m,'early-slots-80',many_slots(80),early=True);v=value(data,'%v')
 check('flag-collapses',v['top'],v)
 _,err=solve(m,'default-silent',unknown_extent(4)[0]);check('default-unlabelled','NOT the reference analysis' not in err,err[:200])
 result={'major':m,'cases':len(results),'passed':sum(r['ok'] for r in results),'results':results}
 (R/f'.tmp/reference-k-{m}.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,indent=1))
 print(json.dumps({k:result[k] for k in ('major','cases','passed')}|{'failed':[r for r in results if not r['ok']]}))
 sys.exit(0 if result['passed']==result['cases'] else 1)
if __name__=='__main__':main()
      }
    }
    memos.push_back(&memo);
  }
  // When no group memo was rebuilt since the whole result, every group only grew: the whole
  // load is its last result plus the groups whose results changed. Otherwise a full union.
  bool grown=whole.keep.uid()!=0 && whole.parts.size()==memos.size();
  for(size_t n=0;grown && n<memos.size();++n)grown=whole.parts[n].second==memos[n]->rebuilds;
  AV::startAbsorbing();
  if(grown) {
    r.absorb(whole.result);
    for(size_t n=0;n<memos.size();++n)if(whole.parts[n].first!=memos[n]->result.atoms.uid()*2+memos[n]->result.top)r.absorb(memos[n]->result);
  } else for(auto *memo:memos)r.absorb(memo->result);
  r.settle();
  // Loading created slots only when seeding on demand, which is not a version change.
  uint64_t after=0;
  for(auto &group:groups)if(group.first)after+=objectVersion[group.first];
  if(!p.top) {
    whole.result=r;whole.versions=after;whole.keep=p.atoms;whole.parts.clear();
    for(auto *memo:memos)whole.parts.push_back({memo->result.atoms.uid()*2+memo->result.top,memo->rebuilds});
  }
  return r;
}
std::set<Function *> Analysis::candidates(Value *v, FunctionType *type) {
  AV a = get(v);
  std::set<Function *> r;
  for (auto &x : a.atoms)
    if (x.kind == Atom::Fn)
      if (auto *f = resolve(const_cast<Value *>(x.object)))
        r.insert(f);
  bool top = a.top;
  for (auto &x : a.atoms)
    top |= x.kind == Atom::Ptr;
  if (top) {
    if(currentStep>=0) {
      // The module is frozen while solving; use lists need not be rescanned per site.
      if(!addressTakenKnown) {
        addressTakenKnown=true;
        for(Function &f:M)if(f.hasAddressTaken() && !markers().count(f.getName().str()))addressTaken.push_back(&f);
      }
      for(auto *f:addressTaken)if(!type || f->getFunctionType()==type)r.insert(resolve(f));
    } else for (Function &f : M)
      if (f.hasAddressTaken() && (!type || f.getFunctionType() == type) &&
          !markers().count(f.getName().str()))
        r.insert(resolve(&f));
  }
  return r;
}
struct Leaf {
  std::vector<unsigned> path;
  uint64_t offset;
        value=atom(Atom::Ptr,nullptr,0,-1);
        if(boundary)value.merge(atom(Atom::Root,&arg));
      } else value.top=true;
      setField(&arg,leaf.path,value);
    }
  };
  // Host reachability, address uses and device boundaries grow together with
  // PT9 and region candidates. An address used only by device code is not a
  // host seed. Region edges start a new entry and do not inherit H.
  auto seeds=[&]() {
    for(auto *f:host) {
      std::set<Value *> visited;
      std::function<void(Value *)> use=[&](Value *v) {
        if(!visited.insert(v).second)return;
        if(auto *g=resolve(v)) {if(!g->isDeclaration())changed|=host.insert(g).second;return;}
        if(auto *g=dyn_cast<GlobalVariable>(v)) {if(g->hasInitializer())use(g->getInitializer());return;}
        if(auto *c=dyn_cast<Constant>(v))for(Value *operand:c->operands())use(operand);
      };
      bool markersHere=false;
      for(auto &i:instructions(f)) {
        auto *call=dyn_cast<CallBase>(&i);auto *callee=call?call->getCalledFunction():nullptr;
        if(callee && markers().count(callee->getName().str())) {markersHere=true;continue;}
        for(Value *operand:i.operands())use(operand);
      }
      for(auto *g:edges[f])changed|=host.insert(g).second;
      if(markersHere)changed|=entries.insert(f).second;
      // A non-local launch boundary may only stash its handle for a later
      // entry. Seed that candidate ROOT before solving the cross-entry memory
      // paths; §6.6 pruning removes it unless it actually reaches a handle use.
      seedParameters(f,entries.count(f) || !f->hasLocalLinkage());
    }
    for(Function &f:M)for(auto &i:instructions(f))if(auto *call=dyn_cast<CallBase>(&i))
      if(auto *g=call->getCalledFunction())for(unsigned p:regionOperands(g->getName()))
        for(auto *candidate:candidates(call->getArgOperand(p)))
          if(!candidate->isDeclaration())changed|=entries.insert(candidate).second;
    for(auto *f:entries)seedParameters(f,!(ReversibleEntry && f==Entry));
  };
  std::vector<Instruction *> order;
  for(Function &f:M)for(Instruction &i:instructions(f))order.push_back(&i);
  dirty.assign(order.size(),1);
  auto solveSeeds=[&]() {
    // Host reach and region entries read the whole module; they change only with
    // call edges or with values at quiescence, and are idempotent once settled.
    do {changed=false;seeds();} while(changed);
    seedsDue=false;
  };
  if(AnalysisTrace)errs()<<"bennett: analysis: "<<order.size()<<" instructions, "<<slots.size()<<" seed slots, prepass "<<std::chrono::duration_cast<std::chrono::milliseconds>(std::chrono::steady_clock::now()-scope.budget.start).count()<<" ms\n";

The marker fixes the region's **interface** (TDL-1, §10). The body is partitioned by content
(§10) and never rejected for containing classical code; the plugin outlines coherent slices of
action bodies (TDL-3). An **oracle region** is a lifted classical oracle with output targets and
a fault register (ISA §5.2); an **action region** is a quantum action on the wires its events
name (ISA §5.3) with no fault outcome of its own: a fault of an oracle it calls lands in a fault
register the action declares (§10, TDL-8).

### 7.2 Candidate sets and dispatch (maintainer decision 3)

The candidate set RR(v) of a region operand v is computed from its abstract value (§5):

- every `FN(F)` atom of AV(v), resolved by `resolve` below;
- if AV(v) has the ⊤ flag: in addition every function of the required type of §7.1 (for
  `oracle`/`inplace`: every function) whose address is used anywhere in the linked program,
  resolved (decision 3 read literally: "every function whose address can reach a region marker";
  audit B-33). The set is therefore always bounded by the program.

`resolve(F)` follows aliases to their aliasee and **forwarding thunks** to their target: F is a
forwarding thunk of G iff F's body is one block holding exactly a call of G (optionally
`tail`/`musttail`/`notail`, default calling convention, no operand bundles) whose arguments are
F's parameters in order and whose function type equals F's, followed by `ret void` or `ret` of
that call's result; then `resolve(F) = resolve(G)`, except that a thunk on a cycle of thunks
resolves to itself (it is an ordinary defined function whose execution recurses until a budget or
the host stops it; 0.1's `BD-E-REGION-THUNK` is deleted).

**N0 and resolution order.** Before testing the forwarding-thunk predicate or freezing candidates, the plugin MUST apply the N0 passes of §12.2 to the virtual device bodies used by S2; §7.2 resolution then uses those bodies, and §7.3 hashes their materialised closures.

The plugin MUST retain A(S) for every original source function or alias whose address reaches the region operand, dispatching that address to its post-N0 resolved candidate even when N0 removes the forwarding call; a declaration remaining after N0 is still F(8), but an intrinsic call removed by N0 MUST NOT turn its containing defined body into an external candidate.

**Dispatch.** The region operand is classical data. The plugin compiles a body for every
candidate. At run time the operand's value is compared, by the compare tree of §14.2 over the
sorted keys, with A(S) of every **source** S (every function or alias that resolves into the
candidate set and whose address reaches v, plus every candidate itself); on equality the region
`resolve(S)` is entered. No match is the run-time trap `T-REGION` (fault code 4). A candidate
with no definition (an external declaration, a function without bitcode) is entered as a
trapped call: F(8) with a coverage line (§4.5 item 4).

**Ill-formed candidates.** A candidate that is not well-formed for the marker's kind (a type
other than §7.1's, or a violation of §7.5) is compiled as a **trap body** (`T-REGION-INVALID`,
fault code 4) with a coverage line: it may never be dispatched, so it is not a compile-time
error. It is rejected (`BD-E-REGION-TYPE`, `BD-E-REGION-MEASURE`, `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE`)
only when it is the **unique** candidate, because then every execution of the marker fails
(outline E14).

0.1's `BD-E-REGION-UNBOUNDED` and `BD-E-REGION-EXTERNAL` are deleted.

Region operands loaded from tracked memory or relative tables MUST use the reference candidate analysis and its type-compatible address-taken fallback, with no blanket TOP rejection. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-7.2, DLT-12.8-P10, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2, ISA-9.2]

### 7.3 Region identity (maintainer decision 1)

A region is identified by its content, never by its symbol. Candidates that resolve to the same
function are one region. The semantic identity of an **oracle region** in a shape (§8.4) is the
interface hash (ISA §9.2) of `@ref` in its reference object (§16.2): every lproc, data image and
- **No match** (a value that is the address of no function of the program, or of a function
  without bitcode): F(4) `FAULT_UB_TRAP` (outline E13; calling through such a pointer is undefined
  or reaches code without bitcode, which the coverage report names). **Zero candidates**: F(4) at
  the site.
- **Trampolines** (`llvm.init.trampoline(t, f, nval)`, `llvm.adjust.trampoline(t)`): `init`
  stores the tag `0x42454E54` (i32), A(f) and nval in the trampoline memory t (runtimes
  `__bennett_rt_tramp_init`); `adjust` returns t. Before the compare tree, an indirect call whose
  callee value v points to a live allocation holding the tag at offset 0 calls the stored
  function with the stored nest value as its `nest` argument.
- Calls through a variadic function type use §14.3. The same compare-tree construction serves
  region operands (§7.2), handle ids (§6.5), descriptor shapes (§8.4), `switch` (§14.8) and
  `indirectbr` (§14.9).

```llvm example=indirect-table verdict=accept callees=gt,lt
define internal i32 @lt(i32 %a, i32 %b) {
  %c = icmp slt i32 %a, %b
  %r = zext i1 %c to i32
  ret i32 %r
}
# Bennett LLVM Dialect — Specification, draft 0.3

**Date:** 2026-11-01. **Status:** draft 0.3, not yet graded; the orchestrator convenes the council at an acceptance
candidate. It derives from draft 0.2.12 (`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md`, accepted by the sol seat in round 11, unchanged and still
the file the 0.2.12 gate checks). The grading target is Bennett ISA **0.9** (`Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md`, accepted by all three
seats, K1–K81). The dossier is `docs/notes/2026-11-01-dialect-0.3-dossier.md`.

**Versioning.** Dialect 0.3: markers 0.2, handles 0.3, regions 0.3, oracle-abi 0.3, tdl 0.3, effects 0.3, norm 0.2.8, map 0.3, asm 0.2, platform 0.3, driver 0.3, corpus 0.2; targets ISA 0.9. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-23]
The **full version string** (`0.3`) is the compatibility unit (§23): drafts are not compatible with each other. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-23]
Marker signatures, the header and descriptor version 2 are unchanged from 0.2.12; every reference object and hash changes with the ISA version string (ISA §9.2). Producers and executors of the 0.2.x/ISA 0.9 track stay on that track until they migrate explicitly.
Declared dependency markers are retained for historical context; the council withdrew the requirement on 2026-10-31 (CLAUDE.md rule 12).

**Standing rule (CLAUDE.md rule 10; council rubric S0).** This document narrows no capability
that the north star promises (its thesis, §3–§5 and every rung of the §8 demo ladder; Grover
over unmodified SQLite is the core). It rejects at compile time only what the north star itself
requires to be rejected or what a maintainer decision settles; every such rejection is a row of
§22.1 citing the clause or decision. A deferral (§24) postpones mechanics only and states the
capability it keeps. Where the ISA cannot express a promised capability in some case, the
dialect still specifies the whole mechanism, states the realisation that exists for the
other cases, and names a blocker (§25) whose verdict is `blocked:`, never `reject:`. On ISA 0.9
no blocker is active (§25.1).

**Sources.** North star §0, §3, §4, §5, §8; the legality spike and its findings
(`docs/spikes/2026-10-28-legality-findings.md`, decisions 1–4); the narrowing audit (row ids
D1–D25, B-01–B-55, O-01–O-08, G-12–G-16, ISA-1–ISA-11 are cited unchanged); the rung-4 spike

struct BennettPass : PassInfoMixin<BennettPass> {
  PreservedAnalyses run(Module &m, ModuleAnalysisManager &) {
    if (Prelink)
      return PreservedAnalyses::all();
    if (LegalityAudit && !AnalysisOnly)
      fail("test mode",
           "legality-audit requires analysis-only; never production");
    recognise(m);
    checkVersion(m);
    if (ValidateOnly) {
      validateResidual(m,ValidateClassical);

      for(auto &f:m)if(!f.use_empty())materialiseIntegerRuntime(f);
      if(Comparison)prepareNativeComparison(m);
      if(Comparison) for(auto &f:m)for(auto &i:instructions(f))if(auto *c=dyn_cast<CallBase>(&i))if(auto *g=c->getCalledFunction())requireSoftFloat(*g);
      errs() << "bennett: scalar normalisation test mode; full N0-N35/V1-V16 pending M2\n";
      return PreservedAnalyses::none();

    if(!Tier.empty() && Tier!="reversible")fail("input","§19.2 expected tier reversible");
    if(Tier.empty() && (!ProgramEntry.empty() || !Disk.empty()))fail("input","§19.2 entry/disk require reversible tier");
    auto *rEntry=Tier.empty()?nullptr:m.getFunction(ProgramEntry);
    if(!Tier.empty() && (!rEntry || rEntry->isDeclaration()))fail("input","§19.2 whole-program entry missing");
    Analysis a(m,rEntry,!Tier.empty());
    a.run();
    a.checkHandles();
    a.checkRegions();
    if (!Report.empty()) {
      std::error_code ec;
      raw_fd_ostream o(Report, ec);
      if (ec)
        fail("output", ec.message());
      o << formatv("{0:2}\n", json::Value(a.report()));
    }
    if (AnalysisOnly) {

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""PT6 loads update incrementally when an object's slots grow (bp-fj1); the result is the full pass's.

The hidden flag -bennett-analysis-check-delta recomputes every incremental load group in full and
aborts on any difference. This test runs it over every probe and fixture program and over
programs built to grow a loaded object round after round (stores of loaded values, partial
overlaps, strided stores, zero-filled heap images, a written constant image), and requires (a) no difference, (b) the
incremental path was actually taken where the program grows an object, and (c) the analysis
report is identical with and without the check.
"""
import argparse,json,re,subprocess,sys
from contracts import ROOT as R

def select_chain(base,offsets,ty='i8'):
 lines=[];prev=None
 for n,o in enumerate(offsets):
  lines.append(f' %q{n} = getelementptr {ty}, ptr {base}, i64 {o}')
  if prev is None: prev=f'%q{n}'
  else:
   lines.append(f' %r{n} = select i1 %c, ptr {prev}, ptr %q{n}');prev=f'%r{n}'
 return '\n'.join(lines),prev

def grow_global(slots=16):
 """A load of every slot feeds a store into one slot: the loaded set grows one value per round."""
 body,q=select_chain('@g',[8*n for n in range(slots)])
 stores='\n'.join(f' %s{n} = getelementptr i8, ptr @g, i64 {8*n}\n store i64 %a, ptr %s{n}' for n in (3,7,11))
 return f'''@g = internal global [{slots} x i64] zeroinitializer
define i64 @top(i1 %c) {{
{body}
 %v = load i64, ptr {q}
 %w = add i64 %v, 1
 %a = and i64 %w, 31
{stores}
 ret i64 %v
}}''','%v'

def grow_heap():
 """Zero-filled heap image, pointer stores into it, a partial (i32 of i64) load and a strided store."""
 body,q=select_chain('%b',[0,8,16,24,32])
 return f'''declare ptr @calloc(i64, i64)
@k = internal global i64 0
define i64 @top(i1 %c, i64 %i) {{
 %b = call ptr @calloc(i64 8, i64 8)
{body}
 %v = load i64, ptr {q}
 %h = load i32, ptr {q}
 %p = load ptr, ptr {q}
 %w = add i64 %v, 3
 %a = and i64 %w, 63
 %e = getelementptr i8, ptr %b, i64 16
 store i64 %a, ptr %e
 %f = getelementptr i8, ptr %b, i64 32
 store ptr @k, ptr %f
 %x = load i64, ptr %p
 %m = and i64 %i, 3
 %s = getelementptr i64, ptr %b, i64 %m
 store i64 %x, ptr %s
 %t = trunc i64 %a to i32
 %u = getelementptr i8, ptr %b, i64 40
 store i32 %t, ptr %u
 ret i64 %v
}}''','%v'

def grow_constant():
 """Stores reach a constant image through a merged pointer: its exact-byte loads lose and gain
 contributions as the written slots grow (the per-atom path), at byte and multi-byte widths."""
 body,q=select_chain('@s',[0,1,2,4,5])
 return f'''@s = internal constant [8 x i8] c"abcdefgh"
@h = internal global [8 x i8] zeroinitializer
define i32 @top(i1 %c, i1 %d) {{
{body}
 %b = load i8, ptr {q}
 %w = load i32, ptr {q}
 %x = load i16, ptr %q2
 %z = zext i8 %b to i32
 %y = add i32 %z, 1
 %t = trunc i32 %y to i8
 %k = and i8 %t, 7
 %dst = select i1 %d, ptr @h, ptr %q3
 store i8 %k, ptr %dst
 %e = getelementptr i8, ptr %dst, i64 1
 store i8 %b, ptr %e
 %r = add i32 %w, %y
 ret i32 %r
}}''','%w'

def run(m,src,check,tag):
 report=R/f'.tmp/load-delta/{m}/{tag}.json'
 argv=[f'/usr/lib/llvm-{m}/bin/opt','-load-pass-plugin='+str(R/f'build/libbennett-plugin-{m}.so'),'-passes=bennett','-bennett-dialect=0.2.10','-bennett-analysis-only','-bennett-report='+str(report),'-disable-output',str(src)]
 if check: argv.insert(4,'-bennett-analysis-check-delta')
 report.unlink(missing_ok=True)
 x=subprocess.run(argv,capture_output=True,text=True,timeout=600)
 data=report.read_text() if report.exists() else None
 return x.returncode,x.stderr,data

def main():
 ap=argparse.ArgumentParser();ap.add_argument('--major',type=int,required=True);a=ap.parse_args();m=a.major
 out=R/f'.tmp/load-delta/{m}';out.mkdir(parents=True,exist_ok=True)
 results=[]

        auto image=[&](const AV &addresses,const AV &deltas,bool flip) {
          for(auto &w:deltas.atoms)if(w.kind==Atom::Int) {
            auto moved=displace(addresses,flip?-w.integer:w.integer,w.integer.getBitWidth(),false);
            a.top|=moved.top;a.absorb(moved);
          }
        };
        if(xAddresses)image(xp,y,negate);
        if(yAddresses && !negate)image(yp,x,false);
        auto conservative=[&](const AV &addresses) {
          for(auto p:addresses.atoms) {p.offset=0;p.stride=-1;a.atoms.insert(p);}
          numericalUnknown(a);
        };
        if(xAddresses && (yAddresses || yo))conservative(xp);
        if(yAddresses && xo && !negate)conservative(yp);
        // INT - address: PT5's conservative image of an integer operation on a pointer atom.
        if(yAddresses && negate) {bool ints=false;for(auto &v:x.atoms)ints|=v.kind==Atom::Int;if(ints||xo)conservative(yp);}
      }
      for (auto &v : x.atoms) {
        // For these operations the pointer transfer is independent of the
        // right atom. Emit its identical conservative image once, rather than
        // a Cartesian product of logical-stack address histories. Keep the
        // empty-domain behaviour and all INT/INT finite transfers unchanged.
        if(v.kind==Atom::Ptr) {
          if(i.getOpcode()!=Instruction::Add && i.getOpcode()!=Instruction::Sub && !y.atoms.empty()) {auto p=v;p.offset=0;p.stride=-1;a.atoms.insert(p);numericalUnknown(a);}
          continue;
        }
        for (auto &w : y.atoms) {
          if (v.kind == Atom::Int && w.kind == Atom::Int &&
              v.integer.getBitWidth() == w.integer.getBitWidth()) {
            APInt b = v.integer, c = w.integer, r = b;
            bool valid = true;
            switch (i.getOpcode()) {
            case Instruction::Add:
              r = b + c;
              break;
            case Instruction::Sub:
              r = b - c;
              break;
            case Instruction::Mul:
              r = b * c;
              break;
            case Instruction::And:
              r = b & c;
              break;
            case Instruction::Or:
              r = b | c;
              break;
            case Instruction::Xor:
              r = b ^ c;
              break;
            case Instruction::Shl:
              r = c.uge(b.getBitWidth()) ? APInt(b.getBitWidth(), 0) : b.shl(c);
              break;
            case Instruction::LShr:
              r = c.uge(b.getBitWidth()) ? APInt(b.getBitWidth(), 0)
                                         : b.lshr(c);
              break;
            case Instruction::AShr:
              r = c.uge(b.getBitWidth()) ? APInt(b.getBitWidth(), 0)
                                         : b.ashr(c);
              break;
            case Instruction::UDiv:
            case Instruction::SDiv:
            case Instruction::URem:
            case Instruction::SRem:
              if (c.isZero()) {
                valid = false;
                break;
              }
              r = i.getOpcode() == Instruction::UDiv   ? b.udiv(c)
                  : i.getOpcode() == Instruction::SDiv ? b.sdiv(c)
                  : i.getOpcode() == Instruction::URem ? b.urem(c)
                                                       : b.srem(c);
              break;
            case Instruction::ICmp:
              r = APInt(
                  1, ICmpInst::compare(b, c, cast<ICmpInst>(i).getPredicate()));
              break;
            default:
              valid = false;
            }
            if (valid) {
              a.atoms.insert({Atom::Int, nullptr, 0, 0, r});
              // K is reached long before a wide product ends; stop there, as §5.1 does.
              if(++pendingIntegers>=4096) {pendingIntegers=0;a.settle();}
            }
            if (a.top)
              goto done;
          }
        }
      }
    }
  done:;
    a.settle();
    if (isa<ICmpInst>(i) && a.atoms.empty() &&
        (x.top || y.top || !x.atoms.empty() || !y.atoms.empty())) {

# Agent Instructions

This project uses **bd** (beads) for issue tracking. Run `bd prime` for full workflow context.

> **Architecture in one line:** Issues live in a local Dolt database
> (`.beads/dolt/`); cross-machine sync uses `bd dolt push/pull` (a
> git-compatible protocol), stored under `refs/dolt/data` on your git
> remote — separate from `refs/heads/*` where your code lives.
> `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export, not the wire protocol.
>
> See [sync-concepts](https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md)
> for the one-screen overview and anti-patterns (don't treat JSONL as the
> source of truth; don't `bd import` during normal operation; don't
> reach for third-party Dolt hosting before trying the default).

## Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready              # Find available work
bd show <id>          # View issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim work atomically
bd close <id>         # Complete work
bd dolt push          # Push beads data to remote
```

## Non-Interactive Shell Commands

**ALWAYS use non-interactive flags** with file operations to avoid hanging on confirmation prompts.

Shell commands like `cp`, `mv`, and `rm` may be aliased to include `-i` (interactive) mode on some systems, causing the agent to hang indefinitely waiting for y/n input.

**Use these forms instead:**
```bash
# Force overwrite without prompting
cp -f source dest           # NOT: cp source dest
mv -f source dest           # NOT: mv source dest
rm -f file                  # NOT: rm file

# For recursive operations
rm -rf directory            # NOT: rm -r directory
cp -rf source dest          # NOT: cp -r source dest
```

**Other commands that may prompt:**
- `scp` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` for non-interactive
- `ssh` - use `-o BatchMode=yes` to fail instead of prompting
- `apt-get` - use `-y` flag
- `brew` - use `HOMEBREW_NO_AUTO_UPDATE=1` env var

<!-- BEGIN BEADS INTEGRATION v:1 profile:minimal hash:46cd31e7 -->
## Beads Issue Tracker

This project uses **bd (beads)** for issue tracking. Run `bd prime` to see full workflow context and commands.

### Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready              # Find available work
bd show <id>          # View issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim work
bd close <id>         # Complete work
```

### Rules

- Use `bd` for ALL task tracking — do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown TODO lists
- Run `bd prime` for detailed command reference and session close protocol
- Use `bd remember` for persistent knowledge — do NOT use MEMORY.md files

**Architecture in one line:** issues live in a local Dolt DB; sync uses `refs/dolt/data` on your git remote; `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export. See https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md for details and anti-patterns.

## Agent Context Profiles

The managed Beads block is task-tracking guidance, not permission to override repository, user, or orchestrator instructions.

- **Conservative (default)**: Use `bd` for task tracking. Do not run git commits, git pushes, or Dolt remote sync unless explicitly asked. At handoff, report changed files, validation, and suggested next commands.
- **Minimal**: Keep tool instruction files as pointers to `bd prime`; use the same conservative git policy unless active instructions say otherwise.
- **Team-maintainer**: Only when the repository explicitly opts in, agents may close beads, run quality gates, commit, and push as part of session close. A current "do not commit" or "do not push" instruction still wins.

## Session Completion

This protocol applies when ending a Beads implementation workflow. It is subordinate to explicit user, repository, and orchestrator instructions.

1. **File issues for remaining work** - Create beads for anything that needs follow-up
2. **Run quality gates** (if code changed) - Tests, linters, builds
3. **Update issue status** - Close finished work, update in-progress items
4. **Handle git/sync by active profile**:
   ```bash
   # Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval.
   git status

   # Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
   git pull --rebase
   bd dolt push
   git push
   git status
   ```
5. **Hand off** - Summarize changes, validation, issue status, and any blocked sync/commit/push step

**Critical rules:**
- Explicit user or orchestrator instructions override this Beads block.
- Do not commit or push without clear authority from the active profile or the current user request.
- If a required sync or push is blocked, stop and report the exact command and error.
<!-- END BEADS INTEGRATION -->

## Beads Issue Tracker

Use Beads (`bd`) for durable task tracking in repositories that include it. Use the `beads` skill at `.agents/skills/beads/SKILL.md` (project install) or `~/.agents/skills/beads/SKILL.md` (global install) for Beads workflow guidance, then use the `bd` CLI for issue operations.

### Quick Reference

```bash
bd ready                # Find available work
bd show <id>            # View issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim work
bd close <id>           # Complete work
bd prime                # Refresh Beads context
```

### Rules

- Use `bd` for all task tracking; do not create markdown TODO lists.
- Run `bd prime` when Beads context is missing or stale. Codex 0.129.0+ can load Beads context automatically through native hooks; use `/hooks` to inspect or toggle them.
- Keep persistent project memory in Beads via `bd remember`; do not create ad hoc memory files.

**Architecture in one line:** issues live in a local Dolt DB; sync uses `refs/dolt/data` on your git remote; `.beads/issues.jsonl` is a passive export. See https://github.com/gastownhall/beads/blob/main/docs/core-concepts/sync-concepts.md for details and anti-patterns.

 **PPCDoubleDouble priority and rounding.** The reference to the LLVM major's APFloat `PPCDoubleDouble` names its two-binary64 value representation and operation conventions, not permission to violate this section's explicit numerical guarantees: implementations MUST preserve the exact real value high + low for conversions and the correctly-rounded requirements for `sqrt`, `fma` and rounding-to-integral operations, and MUST NOT substitute the legacy consecutive-106-bit fallback when it changes such a result.
 
-For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. +For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with nearest ties resolved as follows. Let L and U be the adjacent canonical finite values bracketing the exact result, with L < U. If either low component is nonzero, compare the parities of BOTH low-component integer significands, treating a zero significand as even. Otherwise compare the high-component integer significands. The integer significand is the binary64 significand including the implicit leading bit for a normal value and without it for a subnormal; its sign does not affect parity. Choose the candidate of even parity when exactly one is even; if the parities coincide, choose the candidate whose exact sum has smaller absolute value. Existing exact-zero sign and overflow rules apply separately. This tie rule applies wherever this section requires nearest-even canonical-pair rounding, including incoming IEEE conversions, FMA and nearest-even carrier transcendentals of §13.4. Directed and nearest-away modes retain their specified rules. NaNs and infinities are represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
 
 Cross-format conversion MUST use the exact sum of both components, be exact whenever that sum is representable in the destination, and otherwise round once to the destination format under the requested mode; it MUST NOT drop a representable low component, saturate an in-range positive total because its low component is negative, or introduce intermediate-conversion flags. For example, high = 1 and low = 2^-60 convert to x86_fp80 as exactly 1 + 2^-60, not 1. The `fpext` exactness statement describes true numerical widening; a cross-format pair whose value sets are not nested still uses this conversion rule, regardless of equal storage width.
 
 Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
 
+Both following witnesses use nearest-even mode (R=0). For u=2^-1074 and h=1+2^-52, fma((h,u),(1/2,+0),(h/2,+0)) returns (h,+0). Conversion of binary128 bits 0x3c030000000000001200000000000000 to PPC returns (binary64 bits 0x0030000000000001,+0). Each constrained operation, with initial flags zero and exceptions observed, raises exactly inexact (16); observed exceptions OR 16 into existing sticky flags. Unconstrained operations return the same bits without modifying the floating-point environment. Negated exact inputs return the corresponding negative high with low +0, again raising exactly 16 when observed. The equal-parity fallback is total: L=-U with L<U cannot be an adjacent canonical pair, since the canonical zero pair lies strictly between any negative and positive canonical sums. Suite DF's tagged `ppc-zero-low-parity` vector checks these midpoint/selector policies and two-zero-low subnormal neighbours; full-domain arithmetic conformance remains a producer obligation.
